Conclusions
•
•

Moderate hepatic impairment slightly increased bexagliflozin exposures (~30% for
AUC). No significant impact on UGE was observed after correcting by the renal
function.
An adjustment of dosage for patients with mild to moderate hepatic impairment is not
considered necessary in light of the wide safety margins observed in the bexagliflozin
development program.

14.2.8. Effect of Renal Impairment (Trial C-424)
Study Design
This was a phase 1, open-label, parallel-group study to assess the effect of mild, moderate, and
severe renal impairment on the PK and PD of orally administered bexagliflozin capsules in
subjects with T2DM. During the study, subjects received a single dose of 20 mg bexagliflozin
186

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

capsules, administered orally after overnight fast. Four cohorts of 6 to 9 subjects were enrolled,
representing individuals with normal (GFR ≥90 mL min-1 per 1.73 m2, n=8), mildly impaired
(60≤GFR <90, n=8), moderately impaired (30≤GFR <60, n=9) or severely impaired (GFR <30,
n=6) renal function. Timed blood and urine specimens for PK and PD analysis were collected.

Results
Parent Drug PK
•

•
•
•

Systemic exposure (AUC0-∞) of bexagliflozin was significantly increased in subjects with
moderate and severe renal impairment, with a mean ratio of 1.34 and 1.54, respectively,
as compared to subjects with normal renal function. AUC0-inf was similar between the
groups with normal renal function and mild renal impairment (Figure 29 and Table 109).
The percent of plasma protein binding was not affected by declining renal function, with
mean values of 88.23%, 87.99%, 89.38%, and 85.97% in the normal renal function,
mildly, moderately, and severely impaired groups, respectively.
Elimination half-life was slightly increased in subjects with renal impairment (7.3 to 7.9
hours) but was not significantly different from the group with normal renal function (6.7
hours).
The percent of parent drug excreted in urine gradually decreased with declining renal
function: 1.25% was excreted in urine in the group with mild impairment, 0.91% in the
group with moderate impairment, and 0.51% in the group with severe impairment.

Metabolite PK
•

•

•

For metabolite EGT0002149, its systemic exposures increased with increasing kidney
disease severity suggesting reduced renal clearance. Compared to subjects with normal
renal function, AUC0-24h was comparable in subjects with mild renal impairment, 1.6fold, and 2.2-fold higher in subjects with moderate and severe renal impairment,
respectively (Figure 29).
As renal clearance is a significant route of elimination for the glucuronide, renal
excretion was incrementally reduced with increasing kidney disease severity, from 32.9%
to 21.5%, 15.6%, and 6.3% of dose in normal renal function and mild, moderate, and
severe impairment, respectively.
As a result of decreased renal elimination of the glucuronide, metabolite to parent AUC
ratios were incrementally higher with increasing severity of renal impairment (geometric
means 69.4%, 76.2%, and 98.2% in mild, moderate, and severe renal impairment,
respectively).

PD (UGE)
•

Consistent with the mechanism of action of bexagliflozin, the urinary excretion of
glucose highly depends on the renal function. In patients with moderate and severe renal
impairment, UGE values were reduced by 60% and 80% compared to patients with
normal renal function (Table 110).

Conclusion
•
•

Bexagliflozin exposures increased with increasing severity of renal impairment, with a

FDA Reviewer Comment: Per the Applicant “Because only a very small fraction of
bexagliflozin is found in urine, the increase in systemic exposure with decreasing renal function
substantially implicates the kidney as an important organ for metabolism of bexagliflozin.
However, exposure to both the principal glucuronide and the most prominent oxidative
metabolite increased with decreasing renal function, suggesting that the reduction in elimination
falls more rapidly than the reduction in capacity to metabolize bexagliflozin.”

14.2.9. Effect of Probenecid, Rifampin, and Verapamil
on PK/PD of Bexagliflozin (Trial C-454)
Study Design
This was a phase 1, open-label, nonrandomized, fixed sequence composite study to assess the
effects of probenecid, rifampin, and verapamil on the PK and PD of orally administered
bexagliflozin tablets in 48 healthy subjects (N=16 per cohort).
Cohort 1: Bexagliflozin/Probenecid (UGT Inhibitor):
•

On Day 1, subjects took one 20 mg bexagliflozin tablet; on Days 3 to 4 subjects took
probenecid tablets, 500 mg (twice daily [BID]); on Day 5 subjects took one bexagliflozin
tablet (once daily [QD]) + probenecid tablets, 500 mg (BID); and on Day 6 subjects took
probenecid tablets, 500 mg (BID).

Cohort 2: Bexagliflozin/Rifampin (CYP3A/UGT Inducer)
•

On Day 1, subjects took one bexagliflozin tablet, 20 mg; on Days 3 to 5 subjects took
600 mg of rifampin (2 x 300 mg capsules) QD; on Day 6 subjects took one bexagliflozin
tablet, 20 mg, and 600 mg of rifampin; and on Day 7 subjects took 600 mg of rifampin.

Cohort 3: Bexagliflozin/Verapamil (P-gp Inhibitor)
•

On Day 1, subjects took one bexagliflozin tablet, 20 mg; and on Day 4 subjects took one
verapamil tablet, 120 mg, 1 hour before taking one bexagliflozin tablet, 20 mg.

Multiple PK and urine samples were collected for 48 hours after administration of bexagliflozin
on Day 1 (bexagliflozin alone) and Day 4/5/6 (bexagliflozin + perpetrator drug).

Results
•

•

•

Cohort 1: Previous studies have indicated that bexagliflozin is cleared predominantly by
metabolism to an inactive metabolite, bexagliflozin 3’-O-glucuronide (EGT0002149), by
UGT1A9. When bexagliflozin was coadministered with probenecid, a nonspecific UGT
inhibitor, 39% and 44% increases in AUC of bexagliflozin and EGT0002149,
respectively, were observed (Table 111). Postdose UGE for each 12-hour collection
interval was similar for both treatments, indicating that the inhibitory effect on
bexagliflozin exposure by probenecid had no effect on UGE.
Cohort 2: Rifampin is a strong inducer of CYP3A4 and several UGT enzymes, including
UGT1A9. When bexagliflozin was coadministered with rifampin, its AUC0-t decreased
by 14% and Cmax increased by 20% (Table 111). This was accompanied by a decreased
bexagliflozin t1/2 from 12.9 hours to 5.9 hours. Coadministration with rifampin led to 90%
(90% CI: 145%, 250%) and 54% (90% CI: 119%, 199%) increases in EGT0002149 Cmax
and AUC, respectively. UGE0-24h was comparable between treatments.
Cohort 3: Verapamil is a P-glycoprotein (P-gp) inhibitor. The point estimate and the 90%
CI for both Cmax and AUC were entirely contained within the range of 80 to 125%,
indicating that there was no interaction between bexagliflozin and verapamil (Table 111).

Conclusions
Bexagliflozin is not expected to interact to a clinically relevant extent with other drugs that are
inhibitors of P-gp and UGTs.
Reviewer’ Comment: Coadministration with UGT inhibitor probenecid is expected to decrease
the concentrations of EGT0002149, a UGT metabolite of bexagliflozin. The unexpected 40%
increase of this metabolite is likely because it is also subject to active transport by uptake
transporters such as OAT3, which are inhibited by probenecid.
In this study, rifampin was only dosed for 3 days before coadministration with bexagliflozin. The
current study may not adequately capture the worst-case scenario of enzyme induction.

14.2.10. Effect of Exenatide on Bexagliflozin (Trial C458)
Study Design
This was a phase 1, open-label, two-period, two-treatment crossover study to assess the effects of
exenatide injection on the PK and PD of orally administered bexagliflozin tablets. Twenty
eligible healthy subjects were randomly assigned to one of two groups (N=10 per group). Each
group received both the treatments, alternately, in a crossover fashion, with the two treatment
periods separated by a 7-day washout period.
•
•

A single oral dose of 20 mg bexagliflozin administered 30 minutes after breakfast
Exenatide at 10 µg BID with an initial dose 30 minutes prior bexagliflozin
administration, and 1 hour before breakfast, followed by the second dose of exenatide
alone 1 hour prior to the evening meal

Blood and urine samples were at collected at multiple timepoints for up to 48 hours postdose.
191

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

Results
•

•

•

Absorption was delayed when bexagliflozin was administered 30 minutes after exenatide
injection as evident in the time-concentration profile and a prolonged Tmax of 5.00 hours
postdose compared to 2.00 hours postdose for administration of bexagliflozin alone
(Figure 30). Additionally, AUC0-t, and Cmax of bexagliflozin were increased by
approximately 48% and 25%, respectively, compared to bexagliflozin administration
alone (Table 112).
UGE during the first 12 hours after dosing was lower in subjects administered
bexagliflozin + exenatide (26 g) compared to when subjects were administered
bexagliflozin alone (38 g), with comparable UGE observed between treatments for the
subsequent postdose collection intervals (Table 113).
Approximately 65% of the total UGE was excreted in the first 24 hours for each
treatment dosing period.

Table 113. Mean Urinary Glucose Excretion

Source: Table 13 in Study Report THR-1442-C-458

Conclusion
No clinically significant DDI was observed when bexagliflozin was administered with exenatide.
FDA Reviewer Comment: Rationale of DDI study: Exenatide is one of most prescribed GLP-1
receptor agonists and is administered subcutaneously (SC) BID because of its relatively short
half-life of 2.4 hours. Exenatide is primarily eliminated by glomerular filtration and subsequent
proteolytic degradation, thus, interactions involving CYP450 isoenzymes or enzymes responsible
for phase 2 metabolism are not expected. However, because exenatide slows gastric emptying,
the rate of absorption for concurrent orally administered medications may be altered.
Exenatide increased bexagliflozin AUC and Cmax by 30 to 40%. Given metabolism based DDI is
not likely, the increased exposures of bexagliflozin are likely due to prolonged GI emptying, a
mechanism similar to the observed higher bioavailability from the delayed release formulation
than the immediate release formulation. Nonetheless, the 30 to 40% increase in bexagliflozin is
not considered clinically relevant in terms of safety.
Exenatide induces glucose-dependent insulin secretion and suppresses postprandial glucagon
release and therefore may accelerate the absorption of glucose in peripheral tissue via insulin
action. This mechanism likely reduces the filtered glucose in the kidney and results in reduced
glucose. The observed ~30% reduction in first 0- to 12-hour UGE when bexagliflozin was
administered with exenatide may not be clinically relevant.

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

14.2.11. Effect of Bexagliflozin on the PK of
Metformin, Glimepiride or Sitagliptin (Trial C453)
Study Design
This was a phase 1, open-label, three-period, three-treatment crossover study designed to assess
the effects of bexagliflozin on the PK of metformin, glimepiride, and sitagliptin. Fifty-four
eligible healthy subjects were randomly assigned to one of three groups (N=18 per group).
•
•
•

Group 1: bexagliflozin /metformin
Group 2: bexagliflozin /glimepiride
Group 3: bexagliflozin /sitagliptin

For each group, every subject received a single dose of bexagliflozin tablet, 20 mg, alone, a
single dose of an oral hypoglycemic agent (OHA: 1000 mg metformin, 2 mg glimepiride, or
100 mg sitagliptin) alone, and the combination of both (bexagliflozin tablet and OHA)
alternately in a crossover fashion, with three treatment periods separated by a washout period of
at least 7 days. PK and urine samples were collected for up to 48- and 72-hours postdose for
groups 1 and 2, and group 3, respectively.

Results
•

The Impact of Metformin, Glimepiride, and Sitagliptin on Bexagliflozin PK

The estimated geometric mean ratios and 90% CI of bexagliflozin AUC were within the 80% to
125%, when taken with metformin, glimepiride, or sitagliptin (Table 114). The point estimate
and 90% CI of bexagliflozin Cmax slightly fell outside of the bounds of 80-125%, when taken
with sitagliptin and glimperide. Nonetheless, the extent of exposure change is not considered as
clinically significant.
•

The Impact of Bexagliflozin on the PK of Metformin, Glimepiride, and Sitagliptin

There was no impact on the PK of metformin or sitagliptin when taken with bexagliflozin, as the
point estimate and corresponding 90% CI for Cmax and AUC were entirely contained within the
80-125% margins (Table 115). Glimepiride AUC0-t increased by 27% (90% CI: 10%, 48%) in the
presence of bexagliflozin. The extent of exposure increase in glimepiride is not clinically
relevant.
•

UGE values of bexagliflozin were slightly lower when administered with other
antidiabetic agents but were not statistically significantly different.

14.2.12. Dose-Finding Study Using FPG (Trial C-402)
Study Design
This was a phase 2 study to evaluate the efficacy of bexagliflozin based on FPG in patients with
T2DM. The study included two segments:
•

•

Segment 1: a single-center, open label, ascending dose study in four groups of diabetic
subjects (5 per group) who received 5, 10, 20, or 50 mg of bexagliflozin capsules orally
once daily for 28 days. The subjects were housed overnight in clinic for the first 3 days
for safety and PK evaluation.
Segment 2: a multicenter, double-blind, placebo-controlled parallel group study in five
groups of diabetic subjects (25 per group) who receive oral bexagliflozin capsules at 5,
10, 20, or 50 mg/day or placebo once daily for 28 days in an outpatient setting.

Results
•
•
•

PK: Cmax and AUC were dose proportional over the dose range of 5 to 50 mg (data not
shown).
Reductions in FPG ranged from -24.25 to -41.64 mg/dL in the bexagliflozin treatment
groups and was -6.63 mg/dL in the placebo group. Significant differences versus placebo
were observed for both the 20 mg and 50 mg bexagliflozin groups.
Increases in total UGE for all bexagliflozin treatment groups versus placebo were
observed after one and 28 days of treatment. The change from baseline to Day 28 ranged
from 18.71 to 26.80 g/24 h in the bexagliflozin cohorts versus -0.2 g/24 h with placebo
(data not shown). The UGE values for all bexagliflozin cohorts were highly significant
(p<0.0001).

Conclusion
The changes in FPG were determined in T2DM patients at bexagliflozin doses ranging from

because of highly variable UGE among T2DM patients.

14.2.13. Dose-Finding Study by HbA1c (Trial C-449)
Study Design
The was a phase 2b multicenter, double-blind, placebo-controlled parallel group study to assess
the effect of once daily bexagliflozin at doses ranging from 5-mg to 20-mg tablets for 12 weeks
on HbA1c in either treatment-naïve T2DM subjects or in subjects who were treated with one oral
antidiabetic agent. The primary efficacy endpoint was change from baseline in HbA1c after 12
weeks of placebo or bexagliflozin treatment. The secondary efficacy endpoint included FPG,
seated systolic blood pressure (SBP) and diastolic blood pressure (DBP) and body weight.
Figure 33. Trial Design, Trial C-449

Source: Figure 1 of Study Report THR-1442-C-449
Abbreviations: FU, follow-up; OHA oral hypoglycemic agent; Txt, therapy.

Results
•
•
•
•

A dose-dependent reduction in HbA1c over 12 weeks was observed with bexagliflozin
doses ranging from 5 mg to 20 mg (Figure 34). All decreases in percent HbA1c were
statistically significant and clinically meaningful.
The proportions of subjects who achieved a postbaseline HbA1c <7% were 15% in
placebo and 21%, 22%, and 36% in bexagliflozin 5 mg, 10 mg, and 20 mg treatment
groups, respectively.
A dose-dependent decrease in FPG from baseline to Week 12 was observed with the
maximum mean decrease of -1.240 (SD 1.2305) mmol/L in the bexagliflozin 20 mg
group.
Daily dosing of bexagliflozin tablets for 12 weeks was well tolerated. Among all groups,
subjects with related TEAEs were higher in the 20 mg bexagliflozin group (22.4%)
compared to the related events in the 5 mg bexagliflozin (8.3%), 10 mg bexagliflozin
(6.9%), or placebo (8.3%) groups (Table 117)

14.2.14. Effects on QT Prolongation (Trial C-428)

interval between each drug administration. All three treatments were administered in the fasted
state. Blood samples were collected for up to 24 hours.

14.3. Pharmacometrics Review
14.3.1. Review Summary
In general, the Applicant’s population pharmacokinetic (popPK) analysis is considered
acceptable for describing bexagliflozin PK (sustained-release or SR formulation for
commercialization) in healthy subjects and those with T2DM. The final popPK model is
adequate for assessing the impact of clinically relevant covariates on drug exposure and
supporting labeling statements. The Applicant’s popPK analyses were verified by the reviewer,
with no significant discordance identified. More specifically, the developed covariate model was
used to support the current submission as outlined in Table 119.

•
•

parameters

(b) (4)

categorical variable was not included in the final popPK model (refer to Section 5.2 of

14.3.3. Model Development
Data
The popPK analysis was based on pooled PK data from 12 studies that administered the SR
formulation. The pooled database included healthy volunteers, diabetic subjects (with or without
essential hypertension), and subjects with hepatic impairment. The final NONMEM dataset for
analysis contained 6247 PK observations from 884 unique subjects. Brief descriptions of the
studies included are presented in Table 120. Table 121 provides a summary of the baseline
demographics of the popPK populations.

Base Model
A base 2-compartment model with first-order [absorption (Ka) with lag time (Tlag)] and firstorder elimination (CL) was developed to characterize bexagliflozin disposition. A schematic of
the base structural model is shown in Figure 37.
Interindividual variability (IIV) was modeled on primary PK parameters for patient level random
effects in the base model. Residual variability was described using the proportional error model.
Model selection from the base model was guided by change in objective function (OBJ), residual
variance, IIV, and examination of diagnostic (e.g., goodness-of-fit) plots. Covariates for the final
model were selected using the forward addition and backwards elimination approach using the
base structural model.
Figure 37. Scheme of Base Model Structure

Source: Applicant’s PopPK Report THR-1442-C-447, Figure S1, page 4
Abbreviations: CL, clearance; Ka, absorption constant; Q, inter-compartmental clearance; Vc, central volume of distribution; Vp,
peripheral volume of distribution.

FDA Reviewer Comment: The Applicant also tested the same structural model with transit
compartments and considered it a better base model (without covariates); however, the 2compartment model with Ka and Tlag appeared to describe the pooled PK of bexagliflozin
adequately via diagnostic plots (reviewer noted obvious trends for creatinine clearance [CrCL]
and body weight when plotted against ETA for CL, indicating further model refinement was
needed). As such, the Applicant noted that structural model with Ka and Tlag was used for
covariate selection because of less computational intensity, and final covariate model refinement
was conducted with the transit model.

Covariate Analysis
Covariates shown in Table 122 were evaluated in a forward-selection manner, and candidate
models were evaluated based on OBJ reductions and PK parameter precision. The full model
underwent backwards elimination. In addition, transit compartments were employed to describe
the absorption profile as a candidate model, and an OMEGA block between CL and central
compartment was also tested for the final covariate model. Refer to the Applicant’s popPK report
Section 5.2 for details on candidate models and results of backwards elimination.

FDA Reviewer Comment: VPC plots (fed state not shown; refer to Applicant’s popPK report)
demonstrate that the model has slight overpredictions, and this is more notable for the lower
prediction intervals (lower purple shaded areas). Generally, the central tendency of the PK data
are adequately captured as shown in the pink median prediction intervals. VPC was also
performed for bexagliflozin 20 mg with fasted condition by categorical body weight (≤80 kg
versus >80 kg) and by CrCL (≤100 mL/min versus >100 mL/min) and no obvious
misspecification was identified (figures not shown; refer to figures 21 and 22 in Applicant’s
popPK report). Overall, the population PK model is adequate in characterizing the disposition
of bexagliflozin at 20 mg and in evaluating impacts of clinical covariates. Based on the
Applicant’s assessment of impacts of covariates and typical population factors on bexagliflozin
exposure, no clinically meaningful difference was identified with regard to steady-state AUC,
Cmax, or Cmin.

FDA Reviewer Comment: While food was significant and included in the popPK model, the
estimated food effect (reduction in exposure with food versus fasted state) may be confounded by
sparse sampling and other uncontrolled factors. Food effect was evaluated in a phase 1
crossover, dedicated food effect study (Trial C-481) in healthy volunteers. For food effect, refer
to Therapeutic Individualization in Section 8.2.
The Applicant performed simulations (N=500 for each population) to further assess covariate
effects with the final popPK model. For each simulated population, the covariates were set to
mean population baseline values (see footnote in Figure 44). Since patient populations were not
significant in the final model (based on stepwise covariate selection), simulation results for
patients with hypertension or T2DM with hypertension were similar with T2DM below. The
Applicant concludes that although Asian patients have slightly higher AUC and Cmax than those
of the Caucasian patients, Cmin are overall similar. Generally, the ranges of exposure metrics
overlap and no apparent differences were observed.

14.3.5. Reviewer’s Independent Analysis
14.3.5.1. Introduction
Trial C-449 was a phase 2b, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group doseranging study in subjects with type 2 diabetes mellitus. The primary endpoint was to: 1) compare
HbA1c reduction from baseline in treatment groups receiving 5 mg, 10 mg, or 20 mg
216

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

bexagliflozin to placebo group over 12 weeks, and 2) identify dose(s) for late-stage clinical trials.
This analysis aims to describe the dose-response efficacy relationship (and the associated
parameter variability) in T2DM subjects in Trial C-449.
14.3.5.2. Objectives
Analysis objectives are:
•
•

To identify the dose-response relationship of bexagliflozin
To quantify the percent of pharmacological effect achieved with proposed dosage
14.3.5.3. Methods

Analysis was conducted in R (version 3.6.3; R Core Team) and DoseFinding, an R package
(Bjoern). After excluding the missing efficacy endpoints, a nonlinear model (dose-response Emax
model) was fitted to the observed change in HbA1c (n=256) at Week 12 for the full analysis
population using the fitMod function. Standard errors of the parameter estimates were used to
generate the 95% confidence interval.
To obtain the placebo-adjusted percent of maximal pharmacological effect achieved at
bexagliflozin 20 mg, a sensitivity analysis was conducted with placebo mean change set to zero.
The analysis dataset is summarized in Table 125.
Table 125. Analysis Dataset
Trial Number
Name
C-449
adeff.xpt

Link to EDR
\\CDSESUB1\EVSPROD\nda214373\0001\m5\datasets\thr1442-c-449\analysis\adam\datasets\adeff.xpt

Source: Submitted by Sponsor in NDA in Module 5.

14.3.5.4. Results
After excluding missing data, 265 subjects were included in the analysis. Table 126 provides a
summary of the sample size and efficacy endpoint for the analysis. Figure 45 illustrates the Emax
model fit and the associated 95% CI based on parameter standard errors. The estimated half
maximal effective dose was 2.96 mg (SE, 2.2) and Emax was -0.82% (SE, 0.16).

14.4. Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation
and Performance
Bioanalytical Methods for the Measurement of Plasma Bexagliflozin
LC-MS/MS methods were developed and validated to determine concentrations of bexagliflozin
and its metabolites in human plasma collected in the completed clinical studies. Detailed
methods and validation parameters are described below (Table 128 to Table 132). The linearity
of standard curve, selectivity/specificity, assay accuracy and precision, extraction efficiency, and
dilution fulfilled the standard acceptance criteria. The established frozen storage stability and run
storage stability covered the corresponding study period and sample analysis period. The
reviewers deemed that these method validations and sample analyses are acceptable.

15. Trial Design: Additional Information

15.1. Phase 3 Protocol Synopses
15.1.1. Clinical Protocol Synopsis for C-419 (Version
1.0, Date: July 26, 2017)
Sponsor: TheracosBio, LLC
Name of Finished Product: Bexagliflozin Tablet
Name of Active Ingredient: Bexagliflozin
Study Title: A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled Study to Evaluate
Bexagliflozin in Subjects with Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus Who Are not Adequately Controlled by
Metformin Alone
Study Number: THR-1442-C-419
Study Phase: 3
Primary Objective: The primary objective of this study is to determine the effect of
bexagliflozin on the placebo-corrected change in HbA1c from baseline to Week 24 in adults with
T2DM whose disease is inadequately controlled by metformin alone.
Secondary Objectives:
•
•

To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the change in FPG as a function of time
To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the change in SBP as a function of time
221

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

•
•
•
•

To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the proportion of subjects achieving HbA1c of
≤7% as a function of time
To assess the change in total body weight as a function of time in subjects with baseline
body mass index (BMI) ≥25 kg/m2
To assess the change in HbA1c as a function of time
To assess the change in HbA1c as a function of time among subjects who have baseline
HbA1c of >10.5% and ≤12.0%

Study Design: Trial C-419 is designed to evaluate the safety and effectiveness of bexagliflozin
tablets, 20 mg, for the treatment of T2DM in adult diabetics whose disease is inadequately
controlled by metformin alone. The study will recruit 300 subjects with HbA1c between 7.5%
and 10.5%, inclusive, to be randomized 1:1 to receive bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg, or placebo in
a double-blind treatment assignment. The primary endpoint of the study will be the placebocorrected change from baseline to Week 24 of the HbA1c percentage. This is the main study and
these subjects are in the double-blind treatment group. The study will also recruit 50 subjects
with HbA1c >10.5% and ≤12.0% into an open-label arm, to receive bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg,
for 24 weeks. The endpoint for the open-label arm will be the change from baseline to Week 24
of the HbA1c percentage. These subjects are in the high glycemic group.
All subjects are to have taken metformin at an optimal or near optimal stable dose for ≥8 weeks
prior to screening and are to have received diet and exercise counseling. Subjects who meet all
the inclusion criteria, none of the exclusion criteria, and who have consented to participate in the
study, are eligible for study enrollment. Subjects who successfully complete a 1-week run-in and
who remain eligible will be randomized.
Approximately 300 subjects with HbA1c values ≥7.5 and ≤10.5% at screening will be
randomized 1:1 to receive bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg, or placebo, in addition to their customary
metformin dosage for 24 weeks in an outpatient setting. Study subjects will not change their
metformin dosage unless adjustment is medically necessary to manage hypoglycemia. The
treatment period will last 24 weeks. Subjects will visit their study sites at Weeks 6, 12, 18, and
24 for safety and efficacy evaluations, and at Week 26 for a follow-up evaluation.
Up to 50 subjects who have HbA1c >10.5% and ≤12.0% at screening will be assigned to the
open-label treatment group to receive bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg, in addition to their existing
metformin regimen. There will be no placebo arm for the high glycemic group. Subjects enrolled
in the open label treatment group will be asked to follow the same procedures as subjects
enrolled in the blinded arms of the study.
Subjects with persistent hyperglycemia based on blood glucose levels may receive approved
antidiabetic rescue medications. A sparse sampling assessment of bexagliflozin PK in the study
population will also be performed. A net enrollment of approximately 200 subjects is planned for
this aspect of the study. Samples will be taken between Weeks 6 and 12. The population PK
study design and analysis plan will be described in a designated study protocol and the analysis
will be reported separately.
Study Population: Approximately 350 subjects with T2DM will be enrolled in the study. The
subjects must:
(1) have an age of ≥20 years at screening. Women of childbearing potential must test
negative for pregnancy and agree to abstinence or contraception for the duration of the
222

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

study to avoid any possible pregnancy. Females who are surgically sterile (hysterectomy,
oophorectomy) or postmenopausal (absence of menses greater than 12 months) are
eligible if they test negative for pregnancy at screening.
(2) have a history of T2DM with an HbA1c level of ≥7.5% and ≤10.5% at screening, or have
a history of T2DM with an HbA1c level of >10.5% and ≤12.0% at screening
(3) have been prescribed a stable dose of metformin (≥1500 mg per day in the US or
≥1000 mg per day in Japan) as their sole anti-diabetic medication
(4) have a body mass index (BMI) ≤45 kg/m2
(5) be able to comprehend and willing to provide written informed consent in accordance
with institutional and regulatory guidelines
(6) not have recently changed their medications for hypertension or hyperlipidemia (if
applicable)
(7) be able to regularly self-administer medication, as evidenced by consumption of all, or at
worst one less than all, doses of run-in medication prior to randomization (at Visit 3)
Test Product, Dose, and Mode of Administration: Bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg or placebo,
once daily oral administration in 300 subjects Bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg once daily oral
administration in 50 subjects
Duration of Treatment: One week of run-in placebo treatment and 24 weeks of investigational
product treatment
Efficacy Assessments:
The primary efficacy assessment is:
•

The effect of bexagliflozin on the placebo-corrected change in HbA1c from baseline to
Week 24 in adults with T2DM whose disease is inadequately controlled by metformin
alone.

The key secondary efficacy assessments are:
•
•
•
•
•
•
•

To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the change in FPG as a function of time
To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the change in SBP as a function of time
To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the change in HbA1c as a function of time
To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the change in total body weight as a function of
time in subjects with baseline BMI ≥25 kg/m2
To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the proportion of subjects achieving an HbA1c
≤7.0% over time
To assess the change in HbA1c as a function of time among subjects who have baseline
HbA1c level of ≥7.5% and ≤10.5%
To measure the change from baseline to Week 24 in subjects with baseline HbA1c level
of >10.5% and ≤12.0%

Other assessment: Evaluation of bexagliflozin pharmacokinetics
Safety Assessments: Adverse events record, laboratory data including hematology, serum
chemistry, urinalysis, electrocardiogram (ECG), vital signs, physical examinations, and
concomitant medication use.
Statistical Methods: Summaries will be presented for double-blinded portion of the study,
separately from the 50-subject open-label substudy. No formal hypothesis testing will be
223

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

performed for the substudy. Data summaries will report descriptive statistics (number of subjects
[n], mean, SD, Q1, median, Q3, minimum, and maximum) for continuous variables, and
frequency and percentage for categorical and ordinal variables.
The primary efficacy hypothesis is that bexagliflozin reduces HbA1c after 24 weeks of treatment
when compared to placebo. The analysis of the change in HbA1c at Week 24 will be based on
the intent-to-treat (ITT) analysis set using all observed data and an MMRM approach that will
include terms for treatment, visit, treatment-by-visit interaction, baseline HbA1c value, and
country (United States or Japan) as fixed effect covariates. Least squares mean treatment
differences between the bexagliflozin group and the placebo group at Week 24 will be estimated
from the model with the corresponding p-values and their two-sided 95% CIs presented. An
unstructured covariance will be used to model the within-subject correlation. If the model with
the unstructured covariance structure does not converge, an autoregressive covariance structure
will be used. HbA1c values obtained after the start of rescue medication will not be excluded
from the analysis.
The MMRM model is one approach to obtain treatment effect estimates in the presence of
missing data. As sensitivity analyses for missing data, the following analysis steps will be
performed on the ITT analysis set:
•

•
•

Missing HbA1c data will be imputed via multiple imputations, following which the
MMRM will be repeated on the complete datasets with results combined across complete
datasets using standard multiple imputation techniques; HbA1c values collected after the
start of rescue medication will not be excluded from the analysis.
HbA1c values collected after the start of rescue medication will be excluded (considered
missing), and the MMRM analyses will be reperformed.
A last observation carried forward (LOCF) method will be used to impute the missing
observations prior to carrying out the MMRM model.

The effect of bexagliflozin on fasting plasma glucose, the change in SBP, the proportion of
subjects reaching ≤7.0% HbA1c, the change in HbA1c over time, and the change in total body
weight will be analyzed as secondary efficacy endpoints and will not be adjusted for multiplicity.
Safety data will include adverse event (AE) descriptions, physical exam results, vital sign
measurements, ECG results, and clinical lab results from analyses of serum chemistry,
hematology, serum lipids, glycemic control parameters, and urinalysis. The general safety of
bexagliflozin in subjects with type 2 diabetes mellitus will be analyzed as well as the
contribution of bexagliflozin to AEs of special interest.
The sample size calculation for this study is based on two group t-test with a two-sided
significance of 0.05 level. The assumptions that led to the estimated required sample size of 150
per group with 1:1 ratio for a power of 90% are as follows:
•
•
•
•

A treatment effect of 0.4%.
A population HbA1c standard deviation of 1%.
A two-sided significance level of 0.05.
A drop-out rate of 12%.

FDA Reviewer Comment: Metformin is the most commonly prescribed oral antihyperglycemic
agent for T2DM and is often recommended as a first-line therapy for the treatment of T2DM.
The study was conducted in the United States and Japan, and stratification by country (United
224

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

States versus Japan) and baseline HbA1c status (≤8.5% versus >8.5%) was performed. The
protocol was similar to other protocols reviewed in the Division; however, it is noteworthy that
subjects were eligible for enrollment at Japanese sites if on stable metformin doses ≥1,000 mg
metformin per day (as opposed to ≥1500 mg per day, which is a commonly used threshold in
other programs and was used at US-based sites in this trial). However, this would not be
expected to change the overall conclusions from the study.
With regards to trial conduct, there were no amendments to Version 1 and the only modifications
to the protocol were regarding planned analyses. Investigators were trained on the study
protocol and procedures during site initiation meetings. Each center was visited at regular
intervals by a monitor to ensure compliance with the study protocol, good clinical practice
(GCP) guidelines, and the law. This included on-site checking of the CRFs for completeness and
clarity, verification with source documents, and clarification of administrative matters. Seven
site audits were conducted during the trial. One critical finding, regarding lack of review of
laboratory results by the investigator within 3 days of receipt, was found at a US clinical site.
The investigator was retrained on review of laboratory reports, GCP, and site SOPs. This
finding is unlikely to impact trial outcomes.
There were 14 (8.9%) and 11 (6.9%) subjects with at least one major protocol
violation/deviation in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups, respectively. The majority of
protocol deviations in both treatment groups were “IP not taken as per protocol” and
“noncompliance with visit schedule.” The number of protocol deviations/violations was low and
was generally consistent between treatment groups.
Overall, the protocol is generally similar to other protocols reviewed in the Division to evaluate
the impact of SGLT2 inhibitor treatment on glycemic control in subjects on metformin
monotherapy, and the trial appears to have been conducted adequately.

15.1.2. Clinical Protocol Synopsis for C-423 (Version
2.0, Date: April 06, 2017)
Sponsor: TheracosBio, LLC
Name of Finished Product: Bexagliflozin Tablets
Name of Active Ingredient: Bexagliflozin
Study Title: A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-Blind, Active-Controlled Study to Evaluate the
Effects of Bexagliflozin Versus Sitagliptin in Subjects with Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus Who Have
Inadequate Glycemic Control by Metformin
Study Number: THR-1442-C-423
Study Phase: 3
Primary Objective: The primary efficacy objective is to demonstrate that bexagliflozin is
noninferior to sitagliptin by evaluating the treatment effect on hemoglobin A1c (HbA1c)
reduction at Week 24 in subjects whose T2DM is inadequately controlled by metformin.

225

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

Secondary Objectives: The key secondary objectives are:
•
•
•

To evaluate the treatment effect of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the change in FPG
at Week 24
To evaluate the treatment effect of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the change in body
weight in subjects with baseline BMI ≥25 kg/m2 at Week 24
To evaluate the treatment effect of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the change in SBP
in subjects at Week 24

Exploratory Objectives:
•
•
•
•
•

To assess the treatment effect of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the change in the
proportion of subjects achieving HbA1c <7.0% over time
To assess the treatment effect of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the change in HbA1c
over time
To assess the treatment effect of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the change in FPG
over time
To assess the treatment effect of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the change in body
weight over time
To assess the treatment effect of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the change in SBP
over time

Safety Objectives: To compare the effects of bexagliflozin with sitagliptin on the incidence of
AEs of interest. AEs of interest are urinary tract infections (UTIs), urosepsis and pyelonephritis,
genital mycotic infections (GMI), diuretic effects including hypovolemia, hypotension episodes,
hypoglycemia, hepatotoxicity, major adverse cardiovascular events (MACE), falls and fractures,
malignancies, hypersensitivity reactions, acid-base disorders including DKA, pancreatitis,
amputations, and renal failure events. To compare the effects of bexagliflozin with sitagliptin on
general safety assessments including treatment-emergent AEs, clinical laboratory findings, 12lead ECG parameters, physical examinations, vital signs including orthostatic blood pressure
(BP), and use of concomitant medications.
Study Design: C-423 is a phase 3, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, active controlled
study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin in subjects whose
T2DM is not well controlled by metformin alone. A total of 374 subjects are planned to be
randomized in a ratio of 1:1 to receive once daily treatment of bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg, with
sitagliptin tablets, placebo (Group 1) or bexagliflozin tablets, placebo, with sitagliptin tablets,
100 mg (Group 2) for 24 weeks in an outpatient setting.
The main eligibility criteria include 1) male or female subjects with T2DM and 2) screening
HbA1c between 7.0% and 11% (inclusive). Qualified subjects must have taken metformin at a
stable dose of ≥1500 mg/day in addition to diet and exercise counseling for at least 8 weeks prior
to screening. Study subjects will continue taking open-labeled metformin during the entire study.
All eligible subjects will start a single-blind run-in period by taking placebo bexagliflozin and
placebo sitagliptin once daily for one week. Subjects who meet all inclusion criteria and no
exclusion criteria after completion of the run-in period will be eligible for randomization and
receive investigational products.
During the treatment period, subjects will receive once daily sitagliptin tablets, 100 mg, and
bexagliflozin tablets, placebo, or bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg, and sitagliptin tablets, placebo, for
226

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

24 weeks. Each subject will be instructed to return to the clinic at Weeks 6, 12, 18, and 24 for
efficacy assessment and safety monitoring. Subjects will return to the clinic for a follow-up exit
visit at Week 26 or 2 weeks after last dose of study drug if subjects early terminate prior to Week
24. During the study, a data and safety monitoring board (DSMB) will review the unblinded data
periodically and may recommend an early termination of the trial for safety reasons. In the event
of administrative changes or other related reasons, the Sponsor reserves the right to terminate the
study early. There will be no interim analysis; final statistical analysis will be performed after all
subjects complete the study.
Study Population and Main Eligibility Criteria: A total of 374 eligible subjects are planned
for this study. The study population will comprise:
(1) Male or female adult subjects ≥18 years of age
(2) If subjects are female of childbearing potential, subjects must be negative on the urine
pregnancy test and agree to abstain from coitus or use contraception during the entire
study to avoid any possible pregnancy
(3) Subjects with a diagnosis of T2DM with HbA1c levels between 7.0% and 11%
(inclusive) at screening
(4) Subjects whose T2DM is treated with a stable dose of ≥1500 mg/day metformin only
along with diet and exercise counseling for at least 8 weeks prior to screening
(5) Subjects with a BMI ≤45 kg/m2 at screening
(6) If applicable, taking stable doses of treatment for dyslipidemia and/or hypertension for 30
days prior to screening. Subjects do not need to be treated for dyslipidemia or
hypertension to be eligible for the study
(7) Subjects who are willing and able to return for all clinic visits and to complete all studyrequired procedures, including self-monitoring blood glucose (SMBG) measurements
Prior to randomization, all subjects must:
(8) Adhere to the investigational product administration requirements as evidenced by
missing no more than 1 day of placebo run-in medication
Test Products, Dose, and Mode of Administration:
Once daily oral administration of bexagliflozin and sitagliptin with or without food taken
together in the morning

Group 2: bexagliflozin tablets, placebo, and sitagliptin tablets, 100 mg

Duration of Treatment: Eligible subjects will receive a total treatment duration of 25 weeks
including 1 week of run- in medications and 24 weeks of investigational products.
Efficacy Assessments:
Primary efficacy assessment:

Change in HbA1c from baseline at Week 24

To assess the treatment effect of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the change in FPG

Safety endpoints:
•

•
•
•
•
•

Laboratory testing, including hematology, serum chemistry, and urinalysis
Physical examination
12-lead ECG
Vital signs
Concomitant medication use

Statistical Methods: The primary hypothesis is that bexagliflozin is noninferior to sitagliptin by
evaluating the treatment effect on HbA1c reduction at Week 24 in combination with metformin.
The noninferiority margin for mean change from baseline at Week 24 in HbA1c comparing
bexagliflozin group with sitagliptin group will be 0.35%. Superiority will be declared without
additional statistical testing when the upper bound of the 95% CI for difference in change from
baseline at Week 24 in HbA1c comparing bexagliflozin group with sitagliptin group is less than
0. The treatment difference will be examined by using an MMRM analysis of covariance model
(ANCOVA). The model will include treatment, visit, treatment-by-visit interaction, region, and
the baseline HbA1c value as fixed effect covariates. Least squares mean treatment differences
between the bexagliflozin group and the comparator group at Week 24 in change from baseline
will be estimated from the model. An unstructured covariance will be used to model the withinsubject correlation. If the model with the unstructured covariance structure does not converge, an
autoregressive (1) covariance structure will be used. HbA1c values obtained after the start of
rescue medication will not be excluded from this primary analysis. Primary endpoints will be
analyzed on both ITT and per protocol (PP) analysis sets.

228

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

The MMRM model is one approach to obtain treatment effect estimates in the presence of
missing data. As sensitivity analyses for missing data, the following will be performed on both
ITT and PP analysis sets:
(1) Missing data will be imputed via multiple imputations, following which the MMRM will
be repeated on the complete datasets with results combined across complete datasets
using standard multiple imputation techniques; HbA1c values collected after the start of
rescue medication will be excluded.
(2) Missing data will be imputed via LOCF, following which the MMRM will be repeated;
HbA1c values collected after the start of rescue medication will be considered missing.
(3) HbA1c values collected after the start of rescue medication will be considered missing,
and the MMRM analyses will be reperformed.
The effects of bexagliflozin on the changes from baseline at Week 24 in FPG, body weight, and
SBP will be tested as key secondary endpoints and will be analyzed in a hierarchical testing
strategy and only if the primary objective is met. Similar to the primary endpoint, MMRM
ANCOVA models will be used. A hierarchical testing strategy will be followed to preserve
experiment-wide alpha at 0.05:
(1) Superiority test of the change in FPG in the bexagliflozin group versus the sitagliptin
group at Week 24
(2) Superiority test of the change in body weight in subjects with baseline BMI ≥25 kg/m2 in
the bexagliflozin group versus the sitagliptin group at Week 24
(3) Superiority test of the change in SBP in the bexagliflozin group versus the sitagliptin
group at Week 24
Additional effects of bexagliflozin versus sitagliptin on the changes in HbA1c, FPG, SBP, body
weight, and the proportion of subjects who reach an HbA1c <7%, as well as the general safety in
subjects with T2DM, will be analyzed as exploratory efficacy endpoints. These endpoints are
considered exploratory and will not be adjusted for multiplicity.
For the primary efficacy endpoint, a minimum of 172 subjects are needed in each treatment arm
to detect noninferiority of bexagliflozin to sitagliptin with respect to the change in HbA1c from
baseline at Week 24 by a margin of 0.35%, assuming an estimated pooled SD of 1.0%. This
estimation is based on a one-sided t-test with 90% power at a 0.025 level of significance. To
allow for an estimated 8.0% dropout rate, 187 patients will be randomized in each treatment arm,
and the total sample size will be 374 subjects for this study.
Statistical analyses and summaries will be performed using SAS® software (SAS Institute, Cary,
NC).
Date of Protocol V2.0: April 6, 2017
Date of Protocol V1.0: February 1, 2017
FDA Reviewer Comment: This study was intended to evaluate whether bexagliflozin is
noninferior compared to another antihyperglycemic agent (sitagliptin) commonly prescribed as
add-on for patients failing to achieve glycemic goals on metformin. Sitagliptin was used at the
recommended dosage strength for patients without renal impairment (i.e., 100 mg), which is
appropriate for this study design to ensure that that NI is not resultant from suboptimal dosing in
the active comparator arm. The protocol is similar to other protocols reviewed in the Division,
229

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

although the NI margin was larger than what has been used in other SGLT2 inhibitor
development programs (i.e., 0.35% as opposed to 0.3%). At the IND stage, FDA agreed with the
margin selection and accepted the Applicant’s justification for the proposed margin.
Protocol version 2.0 was the only protocol used in the study. Each center was visited at regular
intervals by a monitor to ensure compliance with the study protocol, GCP, and legal aspects. A
clinical site audit was conducted at investigational site 1271 on December 11, 2017. Major
findings in GCP conduct included performing prescreening activities prior to subjects providing
written informed consent for the study, lack of appropriate participation and oversight by the
investigator, and failure of the investigator to provide adequate and accurate case histories for
subjects treated in the study. Based on these findings, the Applicant decided to discontinue the
investigator’s participation of the study on March 22, 2018. Seven subjects had completed
screening procedures and two subjects, one assigned to the bexagliflozin group and one assigned
to the active-comparator group, were enrolled and received double blind investigational
products for 6 to 12 weeks. Data of the two enrolled subjects were excluded from the ITT and
safety analysis sets. Exclusion of these two subjects from the ITT would not be expected to
change efficacy conclusions from the trial.
Protocol deviations were reported for 9 (4.7%) and 8 (4.1%) of subjects in the bexagliflozin and
placebo groups, respectively. The number of protocol deviations was low and consistent between
treatment groups.
Overall, the study appears adequately designed and conducted.

15.1.3. Clinical Protocol Synopsis for C-448 (Version
4.0, Date: October 25, 2016)
Sponsor: TheracosBio, LLC
Name of Finished Product: Bexagliflozin Tablets
Name of Active Ingredient: Bexagliflozin
Study Title: A Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled Study to Evaluate the Effect of Bexagliflozin
Tablets on Hemoglobin A1c in Patients With Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus and Moderate Renal
Impairment
Study Number: THR-1442-C-448
Study Phase: 3
Primary Objective: The primary objective of this study is to determine the efficacy of
bexagliflozin on lowering HbA1c in patients with type 2 diabetes mellitus and moderate renal
impairment.
Secondary Objectives: The key secondary efficacy objectives are:
•
•

To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the change in body weight at Week 24 in subjects
with baseline BMI ≥25 kg/m2
To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the change in SBP at Week 24 in subjects with
baseline SBP ≥130 mm Hg

230

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

•
•

Change in HbA1c in subjects with estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) 45 to 59
mL/min/1.73 m2 at Week 24
Change in HbA1c in subjects with eGFR 30 to 44 mL/min/1.73 m2 at Week 24

Study Design: C-448 is a phase 3, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled,
parallel-group study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of oral administration of bexagliflozin
tablets, 20 mg compared to placebo in subjects with T2DM, moderate renal impairment, and
inadequate glycemic control. Main eligibility criteria include: 1) male or female subjects with
T2DM, 2) screening HbA1c of 7.0 to 10.5% (inclusive), and 3) eGFR based on serum creatinine
at 2 different assessments ≥30 to <60 mL/min/1.73 m2. The eGFR will be calculated by the
modification of diet in renal disease study equation (MDRD). At the time of screening, the doses
and frequency of all antidiabetic medications must have been stable for 8 weeks.
All eligible subjects will continue their prescreening antidiabetic medications and start a 1-week
placebo run-in period. Subjects who are compliant in taking run-in medication (missing no more
than one dose) will be eligible for randomization and receive study drug. Approximately 300
subjects (150 per arm) will be randomly assigned to receive bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg or
placebo, in equal ratio once daily for 24 weeks in an outpatient setting. Randomization will be
stratified by screening HbA1c level (7.0 to 8.5% or 8.6 to 10.5%), screening antidiabetic
treatment regimen (insulin treated or other) and screening eGFR (30 to 44 mL/min/1.73 m2 or 45
to 59 mL/min/1.73 m2). A minimum of 135 subjects must be in the eGFR 30 to 44 mL/min/1.73
m2 stratum.
Subjects with hyperglycemia based on blood glucose levels may receive approved antidiabetic
medication. In addition, adjustment by the investigator to the prescreening antidiabetic therapies
will be recommended if hypoglycemia occurs. Study subjects will have scheduled visits at
Weeks 2, 6, 12, 18, and 24 for safety and efficacy evaluation. At Weeks 2 and 18, the visit will
be conducted by a phone interview unless clinically necessary. A final follow-up visit will be
conducted at Week 26. Measurement of bexagliflozin plasma concentration over time (sparsely
sampled) will also be conducted from approximately 144 study subjects as part of a bexagliflozin
popPK study.
Study Population: The study population will consist of 300 subjects who have been diagnosed
with suboptimally controlled T2DM with moderate renal impairment. The study population will
comprise:
(1) Men or nonpregnant women ≥20 years of age. Women of childbearing potential must
agree to use contraception during the entire study to avoid any possible pregnancy.
Females who are surgically sterile (hysterectomy, oophorectomy) or postmenopausal
(absence of menses greater than 12 months and age >45 years) are eligible if they test
negative on the urine pregnancy test.
(2) Subjects with a diagnosis of T2DM with an HbA1c between 7.0 and 10.5% (inclusive) at
the time of screening.
(3) Subjects who are treatment naïve or are treated with a stable regimen of anti-diabetic
medications that do not provide adequate glycemic control when combined with diet and
exercise. At the time of screening, the doses and frequency of all anti-diabetic
medications must have been stable for 8 weeks.
(4) Subjects who have an eGFR ≥30 and <60 mL/min/1.73 m2 at 2 time points: screening
(V1), and 1 additional time point between 1 and 12 months of screening (the most recent
231

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

historical value may be obtained from available medical records). The eGFR will be
calculated by the MDRD equation.
(5) Subjects who have a BMI ≤45 kg/m2 (inclusive)
(6) Subjects who taking stable doses of medications for hypertension or hyperlipidemia for at
least 30 days prior to randomization
(7) Subjects who have stable eGFR between the most recent historic value and day of
screening (no more than 20% change in eGFR).
Test Product, Dose, and Mode of Administration: Bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg or placebo,
once daily by mouth
Duration of Treatment: 25 weeks: 1 week run-in, 24 weeks of treatment
Efficacy Assessments:
Primary efficacy assessment:
•

Change of HbA1c from baseline to Week 24

Secondary efficacy assessments:
•
•
•
•
•

Change in body weight in subjects with baseline BMI ≥25 kg/m
Changes in SBP over time in subjects with baseline SBP ≥130 mm Hg
Change in HbA1c over time in subjects with eGFR 45 to 59 mL/min/1.73 m2
Change in HbA1c over time in subjects with eGFR 30 to 44 mL/min/1.73 m2
Samples for population PK analysis will be collected and the required plasma
concentrations determined. The PK parameters will be assessed separately as part of the
population PK analysis.

Safety Assessments: Safety will be assessed based on an analysis of the adverse events record;
of laboratory data, including hematology, serum chemistry, urinalysis, urinary electrolytes, and
creatinine; of ECGs, vital signs and physical examinations; and of concomitant medication use.
Statistical Methods: In general, descriptive statistics will be presented for continuous variables,
and frequency and percentage for categorical variables.
For the primary objectives, efficacy is defined as lowering of HbA1c at Week 24 from baseline
for bexagliflozin when compared to placebo. For the primary endpoint analysis, the primary
analysis will utilize an MMRM ANCOVA approach, with treatment, visit, treatment-by-visit
interaction, and randomization stratification factors as the fixed effects terms and baseline
HbA1c as a covariate on all available data, incorporating all visits from each patient at which
HbA1c was measured in the ITT analysis set. All observed data will be analyzed and data
obtained after rescue will not be excluded. In addition, same analyses will be conducted in PP
analysis set (if it is deemed different from the ITT analysis set).
The MMRM model is one approach to obtain treatment effect estimates in the presence of
missing data. As sensitivity analyses for missing data, the following will be performed for the
ITT analysis set:
(1) Missing HbA1c data will be imputed via multiple imputation, following which the
MMRM will be repeated on the complete datasets with results combined across complete
datasets using standard multiple imputation techniques; HbA1c values collected after the
start of rescue medication will not be excluded.
232

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

(2) Missing HbA1c data will be imputed via LOCF, following which the MMRM will be
repeated; HbA1c values collected after the start of rescue medication will be considered
missing.
(3) HbA1c values collected after the start of rescue medication will be considered missing,
and the MMRM analyses will be reperformed.
If the PP analysis set is different from the ITT analysis set, the same analyses as described above
will be conducted in PP analysis set as well.
The effects of bexagliflozin on the secondary endpoints will be tested in a hierarchical testing
strategy to preserve experiment-wide type I error at 0.05:
(1) Superiority test of the change in body weight in subjects with baseline BMI ≥25 kg/m2 in
the bexagliflozin group compared to the placebo group
(2) Superiority test of the change in SBP over time in subjects with SBP ≥130 mm Hg in the
bexagliflozin group compared to the placebo group
(3) Superiority test of the change in HbA1c over time in subjects with eGFR 45 to 59
mL/min/1.73m2 in the bexagliflozin group compared to the placebo group
(4) Superiority test of the change in HbA1c over time in subjects with eGFR 30 to 44
mL/min/1.73m2 in the bexagliflozin group compared to the placebo group
Additional effects of bexagliflozin on FPG, changes in HbA1c over time, proportion of subjects
who reach an HbA1c <7%, change in body weight over time, change in urine albumin-tocreatinine ratio (UACR) over time, and the general safety of bexagliflozin in subjects with
T2DM will be analyzed. No hypothesis testing will be conducted and will not be adjusted for
multiplicity.
For the primary efficacy endpoint, sample size calculation is based on a two-sided significance at
the 5% level. Assuming 1.0% as the standard deviation for difference in mean changes from
baseline in HbA1c between bexagliflozin and placebo, an estimated sample size of 133 in each
arm will have 90% power to detect a difference in mean HbA1c changes of 0.40% between
bexagliflozin and placebo. To account for an estimated dropout rate of 12%, we propose
randomizing 150 subjects in each study arm.
Statistical analyses and summaries will be performed using SAS software (SAS Institute, Cary,
NC).
FDA Reviewer Comment: This trial evaluated the use of bexagliflozin as add-on to standard of
care (i.e., not as monotherapy or add-on to metformin) in subjects with T2DM and moderate
renal impairment failing standard of care antihyperglycemic therapy, which is a patient
population for whom an SGLT2 inhibitor would be added in clinical practice. Considering the
variability of eGFR measurements, randomization of subjects based on a single laboratory
assessment could result in inclusion of participants with an eGFR less than or greater than the
desired eGFR criteria. To identify subjects with moderate renal impairment, the protocol
specified that subject must have screening eGFR ≥30 and <60 mL/min/1.73 m2 and stable GFR
(no more than 20% change in eGFR between a historical value and the value determined at the
screening visit). Randomization was stratified by HbA1c level (≥7.0 to <8.5% versus ≥8.5 to
<10.5%), antidiabetic treatment regimen (with insulin or without), and eGFR (30 to 44
mL/min/1.73 m2 versus 45 to 59 mL/min/ 1.73 m2). In general, the protocol is similar to others
reviewed in the Division to assess efficacy and safety of SGLT2 inhibitor treatment in subjects
233

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

with renal impairment, although it is noteworthy that studies differ with regards to the specific
populations being enrolled (e.g., evaluation only in subjects with stage 3a chronic kidney disease
(CKD) versus stage 3a and 3b CKD). The degree of renal impairment and other comorbidities
may be expected to impact study results, and thus, cross-study comparisons are inadvisable.
Updates to the protocol from Version 3, which was the first version under which subjects were
enrolled, to Version 4 were generally minor (e.g., addition of amputation as an AESI,
clarification of selected inclusion criteria). There were 13 (8.3%) and 12 (7.7%) subjects in the
bexagliflozin group and placebo groups that had at least one major protocol deviation,
respectively. The number of protocol deviations were low and generally balanced between
treatment arms. Overall, the trial design, enrolled population, and treatment duration were
adequate and generally consistent with trials submitted to the Division to inform labeling in this
patient population.

15.1.4. Clinical Protocol Synopsis for C-450 (Version
4.0, Date: April 04, 2017)
Sponsor: TheracosBio, LLC
Name of Finished Product: Bexagliflozin Tablets
Name of Active Ingredient: Bexagliflozin
Study Title: A Multicenter, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Parallel Group
Study to Compare the Efficacy and Safety of Bexagliflozin to Placebo in Subjects With Type 2
Diabetes Mellitus and Inadequate Glycemic Control
Study Number: THR-1442-C-450
Study Phase: 3
Primary Objective: To determine the placebo-adjusted treatment effect of bexagliflozin tablets,
20 mg on the change in HbA1c from baseline to Week 24 in subjects with type 2 diabetes
mellitus and inadequate glycemic control.
Key Secondary Objectives:
•
•

To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on systolic blood pressure compared to placebo
To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on body weight in subjects with a baseline BMI
≥25 kg/m2 compared to placebo

Exploratory Secondary Objectives
•
•
•

To compare the effect of bexagliflozin to the effect of placebo on the proportion of
subjects with HbA1c of <7%
To compare the effect of bexagliflozin to the effect of placebo on change in HbA1c over
time
To compare the effect of bexagliflozin to the effect of placebo on FPG over time

Safety Objectives:
•
•

To assess the safety of bexagliflozin in subjects with T2DM
To assess the effect of bexagliflozin on the incidence of AESIs
234

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

•

To contribute MACE+ events to an eventual meta-analysis that is intended to exclude a
hazard ratio of 1.8 or greater for subjects exposed to bexagliflozin compared to subjects
exposed to placebo or comparator treatment

Study Design: C-450 is a phase 3, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled,
parallel-group study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of once daily oral administration of
bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg, versus placebo in subjects with T2DM who are treatment-naïve or
previously treated with one oral hypoglycemic agent (OHA).
The study will enroll male or female subjects with T2DM. If treatment-naïve, they must have an
HbA1c at the screening visit of between 7% and 10.5%. Alternatively, prospective subjects may
be eligible if at the time of the screening visit, they are being treated with one OHA, have an
HbA1c of between 6.5% and 10.0%, and are willing to complete a 6-week washout. Individuals
taking thiazolidinediones are not eligible for the study. A candidate will be considered treatmentnaïve if they have received no more than 14 days of prescription medication for diabetes in the
12 weeks prior to screening. All eligible subjects will start a 2-week placebo run-in period.
Subjects who miss no more than one dose of the run-in medication, have fasting blood glucose
values ≥250 mg/dL on no more than two consecutive days, and who, at the baseline visit (Visit
V5), have an HbA1c level between 7 and 10.5% and a fasting glucose level <250 mg/dL will be
eligible for randomization.
Approximately 210 subjects will be randomly assigned to receive oral bexagliflozin tablets,
20 mg, or placebo in a 2:1 ratio once daily for 24 weeks in an out-patient setting. Subjects with
uncontrolled hyperglycemia based on blood glucose levels may receive additional approved
antidiabetic medications. Treatment group assignment at the start of the treatment period will be
stratified by baseline (Visit V5) HbA1c level (7.0 to 8.5% or 8.6 to 10.5%) and background
antidiabetic treatment status (treatment-naïve or not). Each subject will be contacted by
telephone at Week 2 (Visit V7) for safety monitoring and will be instructed to return to the clinic
at Weeks 6, 12, 18, and 24 (Visits V8, V9, V10, and V11, respectively) for efficacy assessment
and safety monitoring. Subjects will return to the clinic for a follow-up visit at Week 26 or two
weeks after the last dose of investigational product if the subject terminates prior to Week 24
(Visit V12).
Study Population and Main Eligibility Criteria: Approximately 210 subjects are planned for
this study. The study will enroll:
(1) Male or female adult subjects ≥18 years of age at screening.
(2) Subjects with T2DM who are treatment-naïve or receiving one OHA in combination with
diet and exercise. Subjects taking thiazolidinediones are not eligible.
(3) Subjects with HbA1c levels at screening (Visit V1) between 7.0% and 10.5% (inclusive)
if treatment-naïve or with HbA1c levels between 6.5 and 10.0% (inclusive) if on one
OHA
(4) Subjects with a BMI ≤45 kg/m2
(5) Subjects with a fasting plasma glucose <250 mg/dL (13.9 mmol/L) at baseline (Visit V5)
Test Product, Dose, and Mode of Administration: Bexagliflozin tablets, 20 mg, or placebo
administered orally once daily
Duration of Treatment: 24 weeks

235

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

Efficacy Assessments: The primary efficacy measure will be the change in HbA1c from
baseline to Week 24. The key secondary measures will be the changes from baseline to Week 24
of systolic blood pressure and body weight. The exploratory secondary measures will be the
change from baseline of the fasting plasma glucose concentration over time, the change from
baseline of HbA1c over time, and the proportion of subjects who achieve an HbA1c <7% over
time.
Safety Assessments: The safety of bexagliflozin will be assessed over the 24-week treatment
phase. AEs of special interest include genital and urinary tract infections, diuretic effects,
hepatotoxicity, MACE, hypoglycemia, fractures, malignancy, hypersensitivity reactions,
hypotensive episodes, acid-base disorders, renal failure events, and amputations.
Statistical Methods: Data summaries will report descriptive statistics (number of subjects [n],
mean, SD, Q1, median, Q3, minimum, and maximum for continuous variables, and frequency
and percentage for categorical and ordinal variables). If there are missing values, the number
missing will be presented, but without a percentage. All data collected will be included in bysubject data listings.
The primary efficacy hypothesis is that bexagliflozin reduces HbA1c after 24 weeks of treatment
when compared to placebo. The analysis of the change in HbA1c at Week 24 will be based on
ITT analysis set using all observed data and an MMRM approach that will include terms for
treatment, visit, treatment-by-visit interaction, and randomization stratification factors as fixed
effects and the baseline HbA1c value as a fixed effect covariate. Least squares mean treatment
differences between the bexagliflozin group and the placebo group at Week 24 will be estimated
from the model with the corresponding p-values and their two-sided 95% CI presented. An
unstructured covariance will be used to model the within-subject correlation. If the model with
the unstructured covariance structure does not converge, an autoregressive (1) covariance
structure will be used. HbA1c values obtained after the start of rescue medication will not be
excluded from the analysis.
The MMRM model is one approach to obtain treatment effect estimates in the presence of
missing data. As sensitivity analyses for missing data, the following will be performed on the
ITT analysis set:
(1) Missing HbA1c data will be imputed via multiple imputations, following which the
MMRM will be repeated on the complete datasets with results combined across complete
datasets using standard multiple imputation techniques; HbA1c values collected after the
start of rescue medication will not be excluded from the analysis.
(2) HbA1c values collected after the start of rescue medication will be excluded (considered
missing), and the MMRM analyses will be reperformed.
(3) An LOCF method will then be used to impute the missing observations prior to carrying
out the MMRM model.
The effect of bexagliflozin on body weight and systolic blood pressure will be tested as key
secondary efficacy endpoints and will be analyzed in a hierarchical testing strategy only if the
primary objective is met. ANCOVA models will also be used for the continuous secondary
endpoints analyses. Longitudinal data will be evaluated through repeated measures tests. A

236

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

hierarchical testing in the following order will be applied to preserve experiment-wide alpha at
0.05 in the case each successive determination meets significance:
(1) To test the hypothesis that bexagliflozin reduces systolic blood pressure from baseline to
Week 24 compared with placebo
(2) To test the hypothesis that bexagliflozin reduces body weight from baseline to Week 24
compared with placebo in subjects with a BMI ≥25 kg/m2
The effect of bexagliflozin on fasting plasma glucose, on the proportion of subjects reaching
<7.0% HbA1c, and on the change in HbA1c over time will be analyzed as exploratory efficacy
endpoints and will not be adjusted for multiplicity.
Safety data will include AEs, physical exam results, vital signs, ECG results, and clinical lab
results including serum chemistry, hematology, serum lipids, glycemic control parameters and
urinalysis. The general safety of bexagliflozin in subjects with type 2 diabetes mellitus will be
analyzed as well as the contribution of bexagliflozin to AEs of special interest.
For the primary efficacy endpoint, a power calculation was performed for significance calculated
by a two-group t-test with a two-sided α of 0.05. Taking the standard deviation for the change in
HbA1c to be 1%, a sample size of 128 in the bexagliflozin group and 64 in the placebo group is
predicted to have 90% power to detect a difference between groups of 0.5% in mean HbA1c
change. To account for an estimated drop-out rate of approximately 10%, a study design was
adopted in which 140 subjects are planned to be randomized to the bexagliflozin group and 70
subjects to the placebo group. Statistical analyses and summaries will be performed using SAS
software (SAS Institute, Cary, NC).
FDA Reviewer Comment: This trial evaluated the effect of bexagliflozin on HbA1c in treatmentnaïve subjects with T2DM or who were on one OHA and had inadequate glycemic control.
Subjects were considered treatment-naïve if they had received no more than 14 days of
prescription medication for diabetes in the 12 weeks prior to screening. In addition, subjects
were considered treatment-naïve if taking one oral antihyperglycemic agent and willing to
undergo a 6-week washout period prior to randomization. All subjects were required to undergo
a 2-week placebo run-in period. Therefore, subjects on one oral antihyperglycemic agent would
have been without diabetes management therapy for a minimum of 8 weeks prior to
administration of the investigational product. Randomization was stratified by HbA1c (7.0 to
8.5% or 8.6 to 10.5%) and background antidiabetic treatment status (treatment-naïve or not).
These procedures appear reasonable to include subjects previously taking one oral
antihyperglycemic agent in the trial.
The original protocol (Version 2.1) was dated February 10, 2016. Minor changes were made to
the protocol in Version 3 (change of medical monitor/study statistician, addition of amputation
as an AESI) and Version 4 (subsequent change of study statistician and updates to analysis
plan). There were 36 (26.1%) and 12 (17.4%) of subjects with at least one major protocol
deviation in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups, respectively. In both groups, the majority of
protocol deviations were “noncompliance with visit schedule.” Three randomized subjects, two
in the bexagliflozin group and one in the placebo group, were excluded from the ITT population
