Exploratory Secondary Endpoint – Primary Analysis
There were 313 subjects in the bexagliflozin group and 163 subjects in the placebo group who
had been prescribed a sulfonylurea. There were 7% missing data in each arm (22 subjects in
bexagliflozin and 12 subjects in placebo) imputed for Week 24. HbA1c change from baseline to
Week 24 in subjects who had been prescribed a sulfonylurea with bexagliflozin vs. placebo was 0.39 (95% CI: -0.55, -0.24).
16.4.6.3.3. Subgroup Analyses of the Primary Endpoint
Subgroup analyses showed that the HbA1c lowering effect was generally consistent across
subgroup levels (Figure 64). However, the subgroup level treatment effect associated with
impaired baseline renal function (eGFR <60 mL/min/1.73m2) has an estimated size of 0. This
lack of treatment effect in a subgroup of the population is discussed as a review issue in Section
6.3.1.
To further investigate the potential modulation effect of baseline HbA1c level, a linear regression
“subgroup” analysis using the continuous baseline HbA1c level based on the completers data
was carried out. Figure 65 shows a scatter plot of change from baseline in HbA1c at Week 24
versus baseline HbA1c data, overlayed with the estimated by-arm group regression lines. In
general, in both bexagliflozin-treated subjects and in placebo subjects, higher baseline HbA1c
level is associated with bigger change from baseline HbA1c levels at Week 24. Compared with
placebo, bexagliflozin treatment is associated with a steeper slope, the difference in slope (slope
in bexagliflozin arm – slope in placebo) is -0.21 (standard error=0.05, p-value<0.001). Both the
effect size and the significant p-value indicate that baseline HbA1c level may have a strong
modulation effect on the action of this SGLT2 inhibitor drug in subjects at risk of cardiovascular
adverse events, the higher the baseline HbA1c level, the larger the expected treatment effect at
Week 24.

17. Clinical Safety: Additional Information
and Assessment
17.1. MACE Meta-Analysis
17.1.1. Data Sources
The electronic documents and subject-level data of the prespecified cardiovascular (CV) metaanalysis of the clinical development program of bexagliflozin were submitted by the Applicant
as a part of the NDA submission of bexagliflozin. The Applicant’s report on the meta-analysis
and SAS codes used to generate the analysis results were used for comparison and verification
purposes. The NDA materials utilized in the evaluation of CV safety are archived in the CDER
Electronic Document Room:

17.1.2. Data Quality
The format, content, and documentation of the submitted data were adequate to conduct a
statistical evaluation of the CV risk associated with bexagliflozin based on the prespecified metaanalysis plan. Data from individual trials were integrated into single datasets. The data define file
of the integrated datasets, in combination with the submitted Analysis Data Reviewer’s Guide,
provides sufficient information about the variables included in each dataset. Using the submitted
data, the reviewer was able to reproduce the findings included in the CV meta-analysis study
report, and perform other key analyses conducted as part of this statistical review. There were no
notable data quality or analysis issues discovered in this electronic submission that would impact
the evaluation and conclusions regarding CV safety of bexagliflozin.

17.1.3. Summary of Study Design
During the clinical development of bexagliflozin, the CV meta-analysis was planned in
accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry: Diabetes Mellitus – Evaluating Cardiovascular
335

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

Risk in New Antidiabetic Therapies to Treat Type 2 Diabetes (December 2008), for a premarket
CV risk assessment. The primary objective of this meta-analysis was to exclude a hazard ratio of
MACE+ greater than 1.8 for subjects exposed to bexagliflozin compared to subjects exposed to
comparators. MACE+ was defined as a 4-component composite endpoint with the following
components: CV death (including deaths of unknown cause), nonfatal MI, nonfatal stroke, or
hospitalization for unstable angina.
The meta-analysis includes all phase 2 and phase 3 randomized controlled clinical trials in which
bexagliflozin tablets 20 mg once daily, or bexagliflozin capsules 20 mg once daily, were
administered for a treatment period of at least 12 weeks. As a result, the meta-analysis
synthesized nine phase 2 and phase 3 trials designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of
bexagliflozin. Seven of these trials included a randomized placebo control arm and two trials
included an active control arm (glimepiride in Trial C-480, sitagliptin in Trial C-423). One
placebo-controlled trial, Trial C-476, recruited subjects with elevated CV risk and was expected
to contribute a preponderance of events for the meta-analysis.
In the clinical development program of bexagliflozin, CV events were prospectively defined,
collected, and adjudicated in a consistent manner. All suspected CV events were adjudicated by
an independent and blinded CEC, according to a prespecified adjudication plan.
A summary of design characteristics of these phase 2 and phase 3 trials is presented in Table
192.

Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

As Trial C-476 was expected to provide the majority of CV events for the meta-analysis, the
study design of the trial is described below in more detail. Please refer to Sections 6.2 and 15.1
of the integrated review for study design details of the other phase 2/3 trials included in the metaanalysis.

CVOT: Trial C-476
C-476 was a multicenter, multinational, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallelgroup trial conducted to evaluate the effect of bexagliflozin on hemoglobin A1c in T2DM
subjects with increased risk for CV adverse events. The primary safety objective of this study
was to contribute at least 134 MACE+ to be pooled with CV events data from other phase 2/3
studies in the prespecified CV meta-analysis to exclude a hazard ratio of 1.8 or greater for
subjects exposed to bexagliflozin compared to subjects exposed to comparators.
Trial C-476 planned to randomize approximately 1650 male and female subjects aged 40 years
or older with T2DM and elevated CV risk from any of the following three groups: (1) a history
of atherosclerotic vascular disease, (2) a history of the NYHA class II or class III heart failure, or
(3) an age of ≥55 years plus at least two additional risk factors including: diabetes of long
duration, hypertension, smoking, renal impairment, or dyslipidemia. If a subject qualified for
more than one risk group, she or he was to be assigned to the group with the anticipated highest
risk. To ensure that subjects with diverse CV risks were adequately represented, at least 352
subjects (21%) from each of the three risk groups were to be enrolled and at most 400 subjects
with a history of class II or III heart failure were to be randomized. The definition of group 2 was
modified to include subjects with preserved ejection fraction in Protocol Amendment 8 dated
October 28, 2016, due to difficulties recruiting the desired numbers of participants in the heart
failure group. The definitions of CV risk groups are presented in Figure 66 below.

338

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)
Figure 66. Cardiovascular Risk Eligibility Criteria for the Study Population, Trial C-476

Source: Figure 1, page 17, Bexagliflozin Cardiovascular Risk Assessment Statistical Report.
Abbreviations: AF, atrial f brillation/flutter; CABG, coronary artery bypass grafting; CV, cardiovascular; eGFR, estimated glomerular
filtration rate; HbA1c, hemoglobin A1c; HDL, high density lipoprotein-associated cholesterol concentration; HF, heart failure; LVEF,
left ventricular ejection fraction; MI, myocardial infarction; NYHA, New York Heart Association; NT-proBNP, N-terminal probrain
natriuretic peptide; SBP, systolic blood pressure; T2DM, type 2 diabetes mellitus; UACR, urinary albumin/creatinine ratio.

During a two-week run-in period, subjects were to self-administer placebo run-in product once
daily. Adjustments of treatment for hypertension or dyslipidemia were not permitted during the
screening and run-in periods. Eligible subjects were randomly assigned in a 2:1 ratio to take
either bexagliflozin tablets 20 mg/day or placebo as an add-on therapy to other antidiabetic
medications. Randomization was stratified by HbA1c (> or ≤9.5%), eGFR (< or ≥60
mL/min/1.73 m2), BMI (< or ≥25 kg/m2), and history of heart failure.
The study subjects were to be contacted by the site at Weeks 2 and 18 by phone. At Weeks 6, 12,
24, 36 and 48 postrandomization, clinic visits were to be scheduled. After Week 48, clinic visits
were to be scheduled every 24 weeks followed by a phone interview 12 weeks after each visit
until the last randomized subject had completed at least 52 weeks of treatment and a total of 134
subjects had experienced an adjudicated MACE+ event. A follow-up visit was to be conducted 4
weeks after the conclusion of treatment.
During the first 24 weeks of the treatment period, hyperglycemia was to be managed with diet
and exercise counseling and only with changes in antidiabetic therapy if clinically indicated.
339

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

After 24 weeks, the investigators were to prescribe adjustment of the antidiabetic therapies if
deemed necessary. Any approved antidiabetic medications were allowed except an SGLT2
inhibitor.
At every visit, including the phone interviews, participants were queried regarding adverse
events. Information on all events that potentially represented MACE+ was to be adjudicated by
an independent CV CEC. All potential MACE+ were to be captured as serious adverse events
(SAEs). The SAE listing was reviewed periodically by the CEC members to identify potential
unreported MACE+. All subjects were to be followed by investigators for CV events assessment
during the study, including during the post-treatment follow-up period after permanent cessation
of randomized treatment.
Sample Size Calculation of Trial C-476
The sample size of Trial C-476 was determined based on the required number of MACE+ for the
program-wide meta-analysis of CV safety, where nonexcess risk of bexagliflozin versus
combined control was to be formally assessed based on a 1.8 risk margin for the hazard ratio of
MACE+.
To ensure the meta-analysis would be adequately powered, it was assumed that no MACE+
events would be contributed by other studies. Assuming a true underlying hazard ratio of 1.0
between bexagliflozin and control, 134 positively adjudicated MACE+ events would be required
to provide 87% power to demonstrate nonexcess risk using a 1.8 risk margin, at a 2-sided α level
of 0.05.
Approximately 1650 randomized subjects would be needed in Trial C-476, based on the
following assumptions:
•
•
•
•
•

An enrollment period of 1 year
A follow-up period of 1 to 3 years, except for premature withdrawals
An annual premature withdrawal rate of 6.5%
A randomization ratio of 2:1 (bexagliflozin versus placebo)
A true underlying hazard ratio of 1.0 and a monthly event rate of 0.308% in placebo arm

17.1.4. Statistical Methodology
17.1.4.1. Analysis Populations and Cardiovascular Events
Ascertainment
The prespecified primary analysis population used in the CV meta-analysis was the full analysis
set (FAS) which included all subjects who were randomized in the phase 2 and phase 3 studies
(Table 192) and received at least one dose of investigational product. Subjects were analyzed
according to their randomized treatment.
Per the SAP of the CV meta-analysis, the prespecified primary endpoint was the time-to-first
confirmed occurrence of a 4-component composite MACE+: CV death (including deaths of
unknown cause), nonfatal MI, nonfatal stroke, or hospitalization for unstable angina.

340

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

The SAP also lists the following key secondary endpoints for the assessment of CV safety:
•
•

Time-to-first confirmed occurrence of any event in a 3-component composite endpoint of
MACE: CV death (including deaths of unknown cause), nonfatal MI, or nonfatal stroke;
Time-to-first confirmed occurrence of hospitalization for heart failure or CV death.

Additional exploratory endpoints included the time-to-first 6-component MACE consisting of
CV death (including deaths of unknown cause), nonfatal MI, nonfatal stroke, hospitalization for
unstable angina, unplanned coronary revascularization, and hospitalization for nonfatal heart
failure. Time-to-first occurrence of each component of the 6-component MACE were considered
exploratory endpoints and analyzed individually.
All CV events listed above were defined, identified, and adjudicated consistently across the
phase 2 and phase 3 clinical development program of bexagliflozin. Suspected CV events
occurring subsequent to randomization were reported by investigators and sent for adjudication
by the independent CEC. Additionally, serious adverse event listings were reviewed monthly for
any unreported potential events by the CEC members. If such events were identified, the
information required for adjudication was provided by the clinical investigators and assembled
by the Applicant for delivery to the CEC. The CEC was responsible for reviewing and
adjudicating suspected CV endpoints in a blinded manner, according to a prespecified
adjudication process outlined in the CEC Manual of Operation (Appendix 1 of the CV-metaanalysis study report).
The following censoring schemes were used for CV event ascertainment in time-to-event
analyses:
•

•

•

On-study: Events that occurred during the course of the study from the date of first dose
following randomization (including both on- and off-treatment periods) were captured.
Subjects who had not experienced such an event were censored on their last date of
observation;
On-treatment +7 days: Events that occurred while a subject was on the study treatment or
within 7 days after the last date of treatment were captured; those who had not
experienced such an event were censored on the last date of treatment +7 days, or their
last date of observation, whichever was earlier;
On-treatment +30 days: Events that occurred while a subject was on the study treatment
or within 30 days after the last date of treatment were captured; those who had not
experienced such an event were censored on the last date of treatment +30 days, or their
last date of observation, whichever was earlier.
17.1.4.2. Analysis Methods for Cardiovascular Endpoints

Meta-Analysis Treatment Group
In the CV meta-analysis, all bexagliflozin dose levels were combined to form the bexagliflozin
treatment group (referred to as “bexagliflozin”), and all the comparators were combined into the
pooled comparator group (referred to as “control”). The analyses of the primary and secondary
CV endpoints were based on the comparison of these two combined groups. Note that all trials in
the meta-analysis included a single bexagliflozin treatment arm, bexagliflozin 20 mg once daily,

341

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

expect for Trial C-449, which randomized subjects to bexagliflozin 5 mg (n=72), 10 mg (n=72),
and 20 mg once daily (n=76).

Meta-Analysis Study Group
The meta-analysis was not stratified by individual study. It was stratified by a dichotomous study
group variable: C-476 versus other phase 2 and phase 3 studies combined, since a majority of the
primary cardiovascular events were anticipated to occur in Trial C-476, which enrolled a
population with elevated CV risk. The other eight studies were combined as only a few MACE
events in total were anticipated to occur in these studies of a shorter duration and a population of
low CV risk.
17.1.4.2.1. Analysis Methods for Primary Endpoint
The prespecified primary analysis was based upon time-to-event methods for MACE+, using the
FAS and on-study censoring, which included positively adjudicated events that occurred during
study participation. The following hypothesis was to be tested on MACE+:
H0: hazard ratio (HR) ≥1.8; versus
Ha: HR <1.8.
A stratified Cox proportional hazards regression model that included the meta-analysis treatment
group (bexagliflozin or control) as a fixed effect and the meta-analysis study group (C-476 or
other phase 2/3 studies) as the stratification factor was to be used. The between-treatment
difference in time-to-first MACE+ was assessed by the hazard ratio estimate of bexagliflozin
versus control. The two-sided 95% confidence interval for the hazard ratio was calculated using
the Cox model and its upper bound was compared with the 1.8 risk margin. If the upper bound is
below 1.8, nonexcess risk of bexagliflozin versus control would be met for MACE+.
A Cox regression model with the meta-analysis treatment group as the only independent variable
was fitted to the time-to-first on-study adjudicated MACE+ for Trial C-476 and for the combined
phase 2/3 studies excluding Trial C-476, separately. Kaplan-Meier (K-M) estimates and plots of
time-to-first MACE+ would be presented by meta-analysis treatment groups for Trial C-476 and
for other studies combined, respectively.
FDA Reviewer Comment: Kaplan-Meier curves were provided in the Bexagliflozin
Cardiovascular Risk Assessment Statistical Report submitted by the Applicant to visually
compare the observed survival functions of the primary and secondary endpoints between the
two meta-analysis treatment groups. However, due to the varying randomization ratios and
baseline CV risks across trials included in the meta-analysis, the overall K-M plot may be
subject to Simpson’s Paradox and may be confounded by imbalance of baseline characteristics
of the two combined groups for comparison. Therefore, while the K-M plots for CVOT C-476 are
presented by treatment arms, the overall K-M plots of the CV meta-analysis are not included in
this review.
For the primary endpoint MACE+, sensitivity analyses were done on the FAS using “ontreatment +7 days” and “on-treatment +30 days” censorings, respectively. The same Cox model
as that used for the primary analysis was applied in these sensitivity analyses to compare the two
groups.

Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

17.1.4.2.2. Analysis Methods for Secondary Endpoints

Per the meta-analysis SAP, a hierarchical testing procedure was to be used for the primary and
key secondary endpoints to preserve the family-wise type I error at a two-sided significance level
of 0.05.
If the null hypothesis was rejected for the primary endpoint MACE+, nonexcess risk for the first
key secondary endpoint time-to-first 3-component MACE was to be evaluated against a preset
risk margin for the hazard ratio of 1.3. If the risk margin was met for MACE, superiority for the
time-to-first occurrence of hospitalization for heart failure or CV death was to be tested.
All secondary and exploratory CV outcomes described in Section 17.1.4.1, including composite
endpoints and their individual components, were analyzed in a similar manner as the primary
endpoint using a Cox proportional hazard model, with the meta-analysis treatment group as the
independent variable, stratified by meta-analysis study group. The estimated HR and its
associated 95% CI were to be reported for these outcomes. The upper bound of the 95% CI for
the hazard ratio was to be compared with 1.3. If the null hypothesis was rejected for both the
primary and first key secondary endpoints, the upper bound of the 95% CI for the hazard ratio
was to be compared with 1.0 to conclude whether exposure to bexagliflozin resulted in a reduced
risk of CV death or hospitalization for heart failure in T2DM subjects.
Time-to-event analysis was conducted for Trial C-476 and for the combined phase 2/3 studies
excluding Trial C-476, separately for the secondary CV endpoints.

17.1.5. Subject Disposition, Demographics and
Baseline Characteristics
17.1.5.1. Subject Disposition
The FAS consisted of 4090 T2DM subjects, with 2437 (59.6%) randomized to receive
bexagliflozin and 1653 (40.4%) randomized to receive active comparator drugs or placebo. An
aggregate summary of the disposition including reasons for study dropout and premature
discontinuation of study drug is provided in Table 194.
The disposition of subjects in the combined bexagliflozin group was similar to that of the
combined comparator group. Among the 4090 FAS subjects, 2148 (88.1%) in the combined
bexagliflozin arm and 1442 (87.2%) in the comparators arm were followed until the completion
of the studies, regardless of their adherence to randomized treatment. Across both arms, 500
subjects (12.2%) discontinued study participation prior to the end of the studies; premature study
dropout was similar in both arms in terms of percentages (11.9% in bexagliflozin versus 12.8%
343

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

in control), as well as reasons for study dropout. The most common reason given for study
discontinuation was withdrawal of consent which occurred in 122 subjects (5%) in bexagliflozin
group and 91 subjects (5.5%) in control group, followed by lost to follow-up for 59 subjects
(2.4%) in the bexagliflozin group and 48 subjects (2.9%) in the comparators group. The
percentages of early study withdrawal due to adverse events are comparable between the two
arms (bexagliflozin: 1.5%; control: 1.4%).
Reasons for treatment discontinuation were also provided in Table 194. Fractions of premature
treatment discontinuations were generally similar across the two comparison arms; a slightly
larger fraction (108; 4.4%) of subjects on the bexagliflozin arm ceased doses due to adverse
events compared to that in the control arm (59; 3.6%).

The aggregate summary of study follow-up in the FAS is depicted in Table 196. In the pool of all
studies, subjects in the bexagliflozin arm were followed for an average of 2 months longer than
subjects in the comparator arm. The reason for this apparent imbalance is that subjects in Trial
C-476 had longer mean follow-up time (approximately 900 days) than any of the other trials and
Trial C-476 contributed twice as many subjects to the bexagliflozin arm than to the comparator
arm in the pool of all studies due to its 2:1 randomization ratio. The mean and median follow-up
duration was comparable between bexagliflozin and placebo in Trial C-476 as shown in the
lower panel of Table 196.

longer of follow-up, and around 16% had a follow-up duration longer than 144 weeks. The
CVOT Trial C-476 contributed about 69% of the total subject-years of follow-up in the metaanalysis.

17.1.5.2. Demographics and Baseline Characteristics
Table 198 summarizes the demographics and baseline characteristics of the FAS subjects pooled
across all studies included in the CV meta-analysis and in Trial C-476 alone. The demographics
for the 4090 subjects in the FAS were generally well-balanced between the comparison groups in
all studies combined and in the standalone Trial C-476.
For all studies combined, imbalances between comparison arms were observed for baseline
cardiovascular history and history of insulin prescription, due to the 2:1 randomization ratio in
Trial C-476 which enrolled an enriched population with elevated CV risk or insulin use. Besides
higher proportions of prior CV disease, subjects in C-476 had a longer history of T2DM
diagnosis, older age, and a greater proportion of insulin use at baseline, compared to subjects
from other studies included in the meta-analysis.
In the FAS, there were more male subjects than female subjects (63% versus 37%).
Approximately 70% of subjects were white and about 7% were black, while 17% were Asian and
6% of subjects belong to other races. About 41% of subjects were at least 65 years of age, while
the mean age was about 61.7 years. About 40% of subjects were from the United States. The
mean BMI was 31.6 kg/m2. The mean HbA1c at baseline was 8.1%. The mean duration of
T2DM was 11.6 years with baseline insulin usage in 26% of the FAS subjects.

Trial C-476 (89 in the bexagliflozin group and 57 in the placebo group) and 13 were from other
phase 2/3 studies (7 in the bexagliflozin group and 6 in the control group).

17.1.6.2. Meta-Analysis Results
17.1.6.2.1. Analysis Results for Primary Endpoint MACE+
The prespecified primary analysis for MACE+ was based on a Cox proportional hazards model
stratified by the meta-analysis study group with a fixed effect for the meta-analysis treatment
group using all first MACE+ events observed during the study participation (on-study censoring)
in the FAS subjects.
As shown in Table 200 below, the estimated hazard ratio of MACE+ associated with
bexagliflozin relative to control was 0.80 with a 95% confidence interval of (0.58, 1.09). The
upper bound of the 95% CI for the hazard ratio was 1.09 which is lower than the CV risk margin
of 1.8.

17.1.7. Findings in Subgroup Populations
In this section, analysis results for the primary CV endpoint MACE+ are presented for specific
subgroups in the FAS based on the on-study censoring scheme. Subgroups presented in this
section are defined by baseline demographic factors and clinical factors including age, gender,
race, region, baseline eGFR, baseline BMI, history of CV disease, duration of T2DM diagnosis,
and insulin use at baseline. Analyses of subgroups were based upon a Cox proportional hazards
model stratified by meta-analysis study group (C-476 or not) with a fixed effect for treatment
group to estimate the hazard ratio and its corresponding 95% confidence interval. There were no
protocol-defined multiplicity corrections for these exploratory analyses and as such results are
presented using unadjusted 95% confidence intervals for each of the subgroups.
A forest plot of the hazard ratio and its corresponding 95% confidence interval is provided in
Figure 69 below as a summary of the findings for the subgroup analyses. The results of subgroup
analyses were generally consistent with the primary analysis. None of the confidence intervals
excluded 1. All hazard ratio estimates were below 1 except for the estimate of the race category
“Asian” (1.21); however, the number of events is relatively low and the confidence interval was
wide for this subgroup.

17.1.8. Summary and Conclusions
17.1.8.1. Statistical Issues
There were no significant statistical concerns identified in this review on the design, conduct,
and analyses of the primary and secondary CV endpoints.
17.1.8.2. Collective Evidence
In the NDA submission, data from nine randomized controlled phase 2/3 trials were synthesized
to evaluate the cardiovascular risk associated with the use of bexagliflozin, according to a
prespecified CV meta-analysis plan. Eight of the nine trials are efficacy trials with fixed
treatment period, while Trial C-476 is a dedicated event-driven cardiovascular outcomes trial
with a primary objective to collect CV events to be combined with other efficacy trials in the CV
meta-analysis.
A total of 4090 subjects were included in the FAS for the CV meta-analysis, with 2437 in the
bexagliflozin arm and 1653 in the combined control arm. Among them, a total of 1699 subjects
were randomized in Trial C-476, which enrolled a population with elevated CV risk and
contributed about 69% of the total follow-up time in the CV meta-analysis.
The prespecified primary composite endpoint for CV safety evaluation was MACE+, comprised
of CV death (including deaths of unknown cause), nonfatal myocardial infarction, nonfatal
stroke, and hospitalization for unstable angina. Among the 4090 FAS subjects, a total of 159
subjects experienced at least one adjudicated MACE+ event during the course of the trials, with
96 subjects in the pooled bexagliflozin group and 63 subjects in the pooled control group. Most
of these events (89 in the bexagliflozin arm and 57 in the placebo arm) were observed in Trial C476. The primary analysis was a time-to-event analysis based on a stratified Cox proportional
hazards model with the meta-analysis study group (C-476 or not) as the stratification factor. The
primary analysis resulted in a hazard ratio estimate of 0.80 with an associated 95% confidence
interval of (0.58, 1.09). The upper bound of 1.09 was lower than the 1.8 risk margin preset for
the nonexcess risk hypothesis on this endpoint, and it can be concluded that the analysis for
MACE+ successfully ruled out the 1.8 risk margin. Analyses with on-treatment censoring
schemes, analysis of an expanded 6-component MACE endpoint and each of its component
events support the conclusion for MACE+. Analyses of MACE+ across a range of subgroups
were generally consistent and showed no evidence of increased risk associated with
bexagliflozin.
According to a prespecified hierarchical testing procedure, the first key secondary endpoint 3component MACE (CV death including deaths of unknow cause, nonfatal myocardial infarction,
or nonfatal stroke) was then evaluated for nonexcess risk against a preset margin of 1.3.
Prespecified time-to-event analysis using a similar Cox model as that used for MACE+, resulted
in a hazard ratio estimate of 0.82 with a 95% confidence interval (0.59, 1.13). Therefore, the
analysis for strict MACE successfully ruled out the 1.3 risk margin.
The second key secondary endpoint, which was the composite of CV death and hospitalization
for heart failure was tested for superiority as the next step of the testing hierarchy. The null

355

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

hypothesis was not rejected; therefore, the objective of demonstrating superiority was not
achieved for this endpoint.
In this CV meta-analysis, the overall results were highly influenced by the single CV outcomes
Trial C-476 due to its population of high CV risk, large sample size and long follow up for CV
events. Only 13 adjudicated MACE+ events out of the 159 primary CV events were contributed
by other phase 2/3 trials. This is expected and preplanned. Analysis results of the primary and
secondary endpoints were also examined for Trial C-476 alone and they are consistent with the
overall results of the meta-analysis.
17.1.8.3. Conclusions and Recommendations
Theracos evaluated the cardiovascular safety of bexagliflozin through a prespecified CV metaanalysis of its phase 2/3 programs. The prespecified stratified Cox proportional hazards model
obtained an estimated hazard ratio for the primary cardiovascular endpoint MACE+ (4component: cardiovascular death including deaths of unknown cause, nonfatal myocardial
infarction, nonfatal stroke, or hospitalization for unstable angina) of 0.80 with an associated 95%
confidence interval of (0.58, 1.09). The upper bound of this confidence interval was smaller than
1.8 and therefore met the preset risk margin for MACE+. The stratified Cox proportional hazards
model obtained an estimated hazard ratio for the key secondary endpoint MACE (3-component:
cardiovascular death including deaths of unknown cause, nonfatal myocardial infarction, or
nonfatal stroke) of 0.82 with a 95% confidence interval of (0.59, 1.13). The upper bound was
smaller than 1.3 and therefore met the preset risk margin for strict MACE.
In conclusion, based on our statistical review on results of the prespecified CV meta-analysis,
bexagliflozin is not associated with an unacceptable increase in cardiovascular risk.

17.2. Applicant’s Approach to the Safety Review
The Applicant performed their integrated safety analysis primarily utilizing a pool that consisted
of all phase 2b/3 trials conducted in the clinical development program for bexagliflozin for
improved glycemic control enrolling subjects with diabetes, as well as a subset of diabetic
subjects enrolled in trials conducted in the clinical program of bexagliflozin for the treatment of
essential hypertension (C-603A and C-603B), termed the “overall diabetic pool.” C-603A was
randomized, controlled trial where hypertensive subjects were randomized 1:1 to receive
bexagliflozin 20 mg or placebo for 12 weeks. Subjects who completed C-603A immediately
initiated C-603B, which was a randomized withdrawal trial. For both trials, AEs that occurred
while subjects were randomized to placebo were not defined as treatment emergent in the
Applicant’s analysis. To address this, a modified pool was created, termed the “broad pool,”
which included all studies in the Applicant’s overall diabetic pool; however, only data from the
placebo-controlled portion of C-603A (including both diabetic and nondiabetic subjects) was
included. As the broad pool combines trials of different study design, patient population, and
drug formulation (i.e., C-418), this pool was used for exploratory purposes in this safety review.
In addition, the Applicant also utilized a pool consisting of placebo-controlled trials where
bexagliflozin was evaluated as monotherapy or as add-on to metformin, termed the “general
diabetic population,” which consisted to C-418, C-419, C-449, and C-450. However, as C-418
used a noncommercial formulation of bexagliflozin and had differing trial design features as
356

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

compared to the other trials (e.g., longer duration, lack of a washout period for subjects taking
one oral antihyperglycemic agent prior to randomization, etc.), the review team concluded it was
not appropriate for labeling purposes. A modified pool, consisting of C-419, C-449, and C-450,
termed the placebo-controlled pool, was used in FDA’s review to inform product labeling.
Lastly, to identify adverse events associated with bexagliflozin treatment in the integrated
summary of safety (ISS), the Applicant utilized statistical strategies (e.g., Fisher’s exact test).
The review team did not agree with the Applicant’s use of statistical methods for identifying
safety signals and this approach was not utilized in the FDA’s safety review.
Overall, the FDA’s safety review utilized modified data pools as compared to the pools utilized
by the Applicant and did not rely on statistical testing to identify safety signals. The impact of
these differences in the approach to the safety review is reflected in the modifications made to
the proposed labeling (see Section 21 for a summary of labeling negotiations).

17.3. Submission-Specific Safety Issues
The following sections detail submission-specific safety issues, including the following AESIs:
DKA, diuretic effects, fractures, falls, GMI, hepatotoxicity, rash, hypoglycemia, hypotensive
episodes, malignancies, acute kidney injury (AKI), pyelonephritis/urosepsis, and hypersensitivity
reactions. Hypoglycemia, with and without concurrent use with insulin or an insulin
secretagogue, is also discussed. C-476 was the largest and longest trial in the bexagliflozin
clinical development program, enrolling subjects who were older and with a longer diabetes
duration and more comorbidities as compared to other trials. Therefore, Trial C-476 was
considered the most relevant trial to inform events that may occur at a low frequency, events that
may be more likely to occur with prolonged drug exposure, and/or events that may have a
latency period. Additional trial data are reviewed where relevant.

17.3.1. Fracture
Conflicting evidence exists with regards to whether SGLT2 inhibitor treatment may increase the
risk of fracture events. Bone safety was initially an area of possible concern for this drug class
due to the potential for altered renal tubule reabsorption of calcium and phosphorus, the potential
for altered vitamin D metabolism, the tendency for weight loss, and preclinical bone findings of
hyperostosis in rats. In the approval letter for canagliflozin, a postmarketing requirement (PMR)
was issued to assess the impact of canagliflozin on bone mineral density (BMD) and markers of
bone turnover (PMR 2027-4). To address the PMR, the Applicant was required to complete trial
DIA3010. This trial, which was intended to evaluate glycemic efficacy in older adults, was a
placebo-controlled, double-blind trial randomizing subjects who were ≥55 to ≤80 years of age to
placebo, canagliflozin 100 mg, or canagliflozin 300 mg (Bilezikian et al. 2016). The primary
efficacy endpoint was change in HbA1c from baseline to Week 26, with a placebo-controlled
extension period for the assessment of secondary safety endpoints, including those related to
bone safety. BMD was measured by dual-energy X-ray absorptiometry in 714 older adults (mean
age 64 years). At 2 years, subjects randomized to canagliflozin 100 mg and 300 mg had placebocorrected declines in BMD at the total hip of 0.9% and 1.2%, respectively, and at the lumbar
spine of 0.3% and 0.7%, respectively. Of the CVOTs conducted for this drug class, including
CANVAS and CANVAS-R for canagliflozin, EMPA-REG for empagliflozin, DECLARE for
357

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

dapagliflozin, and VERTIS for ertugliflozin, an increased risk of fracture was only observed in
the CANVAS trial (HR: 1.55; 95% CI: 1.21, 1.97). However, no increased risk was observed in
the CANVAS-R trial (HR: 0.86; 95% CI: 0.62, 1.19), despite the enrollment of similar subject
populations. Currently, the US prescribing information for canagliflozin includes a Warnings and
Precautions statement for risk of bone fracture risk. While other SGLT2 inhibitor labeling does
not include a similar Warnings and Precautions statement, the dapagliflozin US prescribing
information reports that in a placebo-controlled study of subjects with an eGFR ≥30 to <60
mL/min/1.73 m2 with a 2:1 randomization allocation, 13 subjects receiving dapagliflozin
experienced bone fractures compared to none receiving placebo over a 104-week treatment
period. Investigation of this imbalance revealed that all fractures were related to falls and that
7/13 subjects had a history of orthostatic hypotension or diabetic neuropathy. Although small,
inconsistent changes in bone biomarkers were observed, including parathyroid hormone
concentration, calcium, 25-OH vitamin D, phosphorus and magnesium, the imbalance was not
reproduced when all subjects with moderate renal dysfunction in the phase 2b and phase 3 trials
were pooled. A consult from the Division of Bone, Reproductive and Urologic Products
concluded that there was insufficient evidence that dapagliflozin was associated with bone loss
or increased fracture risk.
As identified by Food and Drug Administration Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities
query (FMQ), there was no imbalance in the overall frequency of fracture events in C-476 (4.0%
and 3.9% of subjects reporting at least one fracture event in the bexagliflozin and placebo
groups, respectively, as identified by the “Fracture” FMQ). However, more subjects in the
bexagliflozin group (15 subjects (1.3%)) experienced a serious fracture event as compared to the
placebo group (2 subjects (0.4%)), including serious events of hip and femur fracture (Table
207). The narratives for serious fracture events were reviewed, which did not reveal any overt
trends that indicated drug relatedness (e.g., falls and factures due secondary to hypotensive
episodes) (Table 208). The majority of fracture events occurred in C-476, and other trials did not
accrue enough events to meaningfully inform this signal.
As discussed in Section 17.3.2, there was no imbalance of fall events in C-476, although
undercoding of fall events in the ADaM adverse events analysis (ADAE) dataset limits
interpretability of these findings (see Section 7.7.3). There was a lower incidence of osteoporosis
events in the bexagliflozin group in C-476 as identified by the “Osteoporosis” FMQ, although
data were limited (3 (0.3%) versus 5 (0.9%) subjects experiencing an event in the bexagliflozin
and placebo groups, respectively). The majority of serious fracture events in C-476 occurred in
subjects older than 65 years with baseline eGFR >60 mL/min/1.73 m2; however, the low number
of events in each subgroup limits meaningful conclusions. There was no imbalance in fracture
events in the dedicated renal impairment trial in the bexagliflozin clinical program, although data
were limited (1.9% of subjects with a fracture event in each treatment group).

versus 3.9% subjects with a facture event as identified by FMQ in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups, respectively). Results for
serious events did not differ between analysis method.
Abbreviations: Bexa, bexagliflozin; CMQ, customized Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities query; FMQ, Food and Drug
Administration Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities query

361

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

While fracture events were prespecified AESIs in the bexagliflozin clinical program, monitoring
for these events beyond passive reporting did not occur. For example, the Applicant did not
employ a specific data collection form, any prespecified follow-up investigations in the study
protocol, or independent review. While standard chemistry laboratory parameters were collected,
more specific markers for bone turnover (e.g., parathyroid hormone, collagen type 1 betacarboxy telopeptide) were not evaluated, nor was an assessment of BMD carried out. Lack of
additional information complicates the assessment of the noted imbalance in serious fracture
events observed in C-476. Only one event indicative of increased parathyroid hormone was
observed in C-476; an event of “Hyperparathyroidism secondary” that occurred in the
bexagliflozin group. Regarding the available laboratory data, there were no clinically meaningful
differences between bexagliflozin and placebo in terms of changes in serum calcium or alkaline
phosphatase; however, bexagliflozin treatment mildly increases serum magnesium and phosphate
(see Section 17.4 for additional information on laboratory analyses). The meaningfulness of
these laboratory changes on long-term bone safety is uncertain.
FDA Reviewer Comment: Case narratives for the serious fracture events identified a substantial
number of causes that were associated with high-impact trauma (e.g., snowmobile accident, hit
by crane hook, motorcycle accident, fall from excavator, direct trauma from heavy object) that
appear unrelated to the drug. Restricting the data to nontraumatic serious fractures significantly
diminishes the imbalance. However, when evaluating osteoporosis trials, fractures of the
vertebrae, hip, forearm and humerus (other than pathologic) are generally considered
potentially relevant to bony integrity regardless of trauma severity, and fractures of the hand,
foot, skull or face are typically disregarded (see dapagliflozin DBRUP consult for further
information). Furthermore, despite the lack of data on parathyroid hormone levels and bone
mineral density, the laboratory biomarkers related to bone health that were collected (calcium,
magnesium, phosphorus, alkaline phosphatase) were not consistent with decreased substrate
concentrations for bone mineralization or suggested increased bone fragility. Despite unclear
drug-relatedness, labeling in Section 6 to inform providers of the potential risk of increased
fracture with bexagliflozin treatment is proposed given that fracture events were serious in
nature, including events of serious hip and femur fracture.

17.3.2. Falls
Similar to fracture events, conflicting evidence exists with regards to whether SGLT2 inhibitor
treatment may increase the risk of falls, with only canagliflozin currently labeled for increased
risk of fall events. Specifically, in a pool of nine clinical trials with mean duration of exposure to
canagliflozin of 85 weeks, the proportion of subjects who experienced falls was 1.3%, 1.5%, and
2.1% with comparator, canagliflozin 100 mg, and canagliflozin 300 mg, respectively. The higher
risk of falls for subjects treated with canagliflozin was observed within the first few weeks of
treatment.
As detailed in Section 7.7.3, fall events were found in narrative descriptions of SAEs that were
not included in the ADAE dataset originally submitted to this application, and an updated ADAE
dataset utilizing natural language processing (NLP) found a significant number of additional
events. The reason fall events were not adequately captured in the original ADAE dataset
appears multifactorial, including lack of specific protocol-driven procedures to collect fall
events, fall events potentially being incorrectly assessed as a symptom of an event as opposed to
362

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

a stand-alone event or sequalae of a separate event, and inappropriate Medical Dictionary for
Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) coding (i.e., “left ankle pain due to fall” coded as “pain ankle”
without coding of “fall”). Despite these issues, no imbalance in fall events was observed in the
ADAE dataset submitted with the application or with the NLP-modified dataset (Table 209). As
discussed in Section 7.7.3, there is no evidence of systemic bias with regards to missed events,
including fall events, in the ADAE dataset. Bexagliflozin does not appear to be associated with
increased risk of fall events based on the available data. In addition, the imbalance in fracture
events is proposed for the labeling in Section 6, despite the limitations of the study/dataset.

17.3.3. Malignancy
At the time the bexagliflozin clinical program was initiated, it was unknown whether SGLT2
inhibitor treatment was associated with malignancy. Given this, malignancy events were
considered AESIs. Premarket and postmarket data from other approved drugs in the class
generally appears to support that SGLT2 inhibitor treatment does not increase malignancy risk;
however, canagliflozin includes labeling to inform providers on imbalances observed in renal
cell carcinoma events that occurred in the CANVAS trial.
Given that malignancies typically have a long latency period, C-476 was determined to be the
most relevant trial for the assessment of malignancy. As identified by the FMQ for
“Malignancy,” there were 39 (3.4%) and 18 (3.2%) subjects having at least one malignancy
event in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups, respectively (Table 210). As shown in Table 210,
most events occurred in two or fewer subjects, limiting interpretability. As discussed below,
several categories of malignancies (i.e., lung cancers, skin cancers) demonstrated imbalances
unfavorable to drug when preferred terms (PTs) of similar medical concept were grouped.
An imbalance in nonmelanoma skin cancers was apparent in C-476, with more subjects having
an event of “squamous cell carcinoma,” “basal cell carcinoma,” “squamous cell carcinoma of
skin,” and “skin cancer” in the bexagliflozin group (17 (1.5%) subjects with an event) as
compared to the placebo group (3 (0.5%) subjects with an event). A time-to-event analysis
showed events mostly occurred within the first 18 months of treatment in the bexagliflozin group
(Figure 71). Importantly, the time-to-event analysis also highlights that multiple lesions were
also reported for some subjects, and that there were 11 (0.97%) versus 3 (0.57%) subjects who
had a nonmelanoma event in C-476 therefore lessening the imbalance between groups,
particularly when accounting for the 2:1 randomization.
Risk factors for squamous cell and basal cell carcinoma include fair skin, older age, male gender,
UV exposure, and history of skin cancer. Demographics for these subjects were reviewed, and
subjects were generally Caucasian, over 65 years of age, and male. The anatomical location of
the lesion was generally in a location where sun exposure would be expected (e.g., face, neck,
363

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

arm, etc.). In nonclinical studies, bexagliflozin did not absorb UV light between 290 to 700 nm,
and therefore nonclinical studies to investigate potential photosensitivity effects were not
conducted. Events in the “Photosensitivity and photodermatosis conditions” HLT were reviewed;
however, only three events occurred in C-476, limiting interpretability. Carcinogenicity studies
were conducted in rats and mice, with no treatment-related tumor findings in either species. A
meta-analysis that utilized a systematic search of electronic databases did not show an increased
risk of melanoma and nonmelanoma skin cancers with SGLT2 inhibitor use (Tang et al. 2018).
While canagliflozin is labeled for photosensitivity events, nonclinical studies demonstrated UV
absorption of the compound, prompting additional characterization of potential photosensitivity
reactions. Overall, the clinical meaningfulness of this signal is unclear; however, there were no
events resulting in death, they were not judged as serious by the investigator, and did not result
in treatment discontinuation.
In C-476, there was a minor unfavorable imbalance in events of lung cancer (PTs of “Lung
adenocarcinoma,” “Squamous cell carcinoma of lung,” “Lung neoplasm malignant,” and
“Nonsmall cell lung cancer”; 5 (0.4%) versus 1 (0.2%) events in the bexagliflozin and placebo
groups, respectively). All subjects had a history of heavy smoking, ranging from 28 to 60 packyears, except for one subject where smoking history was not reported. Conclusions are limited
when considering the 2:1 randomization and low event rate.
In the Applicant’s analysis of the overall diabetic pool, they note a minor imbalance in events of
non-Hodgkin’s Lymphoma, with four events occurring in bexagliflozin exposed subjects (0.10
cases per 100 subject-years of exposure) as compared to none in placebo- or active-comparatorexposed subjects. Based on the available data, causality cannot be assessed.
With regard to malignancy signals from other SGLT2 inhibitor programs, pheochromocytoma,
Leydig cell tumor, and renal cell carcinoma were concerns with canagliflozin treatment upon
approval. Currently, the US prescribing information for canagliflozin described an imbalance
unfavorable to drug in renal cell carcinoma events in the CANVAS trial, although the label states
that a causal relationship could not be established due to the limited number of cases. There was
no imbalance in renal cell carcinoma events in C-476 (2 (0.2%) versus 1 (0.2%) subjects with an
event in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups, respectively), although few events limits
interpretability. No events of Leydig cell tumor or pheochromocytoma were observed in the
bexagliflozin clinical program.
Bladder cancer was initially a concern with dapagliflozin treatment and a PMR was issued to
assess the risk of bladder cancer associated with dapagliflozin in the DECLARE trial (PMR
2121-6). As discussed in the clinical review for the DECLARE trial, this trial excluded an
unacceptable increased risk of bladder cancer in the studied population and the US prescribing
information was updated accordingly (see Dr. Carey’s review in DARRTS dated October 15,
2019). Three events of transitional cell carcinoma were observed in C-476, which upon narrative
review appear to also have involved the bladder. Limited data impedes interpretability of this
finding.
Overall, the available data do not support increased risk of malignancy with bexagliflozin
treatment, although it is acknowledged that data was limited. As stated above, there is no known
causal relationship with SGLT2 inhibitor treatment and malignancy.

17.3.4. Hypersensitivity and Rash
(b) (4)
(b) (4)

The Applicant
describes that rash was a concern early in the bexagliflozin development program because one
subject in the multiple ascending dose segment of the phase 1 Trial C-328 developed a rash and
withdrew, and another subject of the phase 2b Trial C-418 developed a moderate severity event
of rash that was likely due to bexagliflozin exposure based on a withdrawal and rechallenge test.
In addition, a scientist working on formulation development developed a rash that was addressed
by use of personal protective garments. All events occurred with exposure to the noncommercial,
capsule formulation of bexagliflozin. In response to this safety signal, the Applicant reformulated
the drug to the proposed, to-be-commercialized tablet formulation that employs a gastroretentive
mechanism. Following the introduction of the tablet formulation, the Applicant reports one
moderate event of allergic dermatitis that resolved with interruption of dosing and recurred upon
resumption of dosing in Trial C-603A. The Applicant states that events of rash/dermatitis should
be considered confirmed adverse reactions associated with exposure to bexagliflozin based on
these findings.
FMQs for “Rash,” “Urticaria,” “Erythema,” Angioedema,” “Hypersensitivity,” and
“Anaphylactic reaction” were utilized to investigate rash and hypersensitivity events. In C-476,
5.4% and 3.4% of subjects in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups experienced at least one event
of rash, respectively, as determined by the Rash FMQ (Table 211). Some events of genital rash
were captured in this query, which may be more likely related to cases of GMI as compared to
366

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

generalized rash events; however, exclusion of this preferred term from the analysis did not
significantly impact the results (data not shown). Events of rash were generally evaluated as mild
to moderate intensity by the investigator, with only one event being evaluated as serious. The
(b) (6) , assigned to the bexagliflozin group,
narrative for this SAE was reviewed. Subject 476experienced events of “dermatitis allergic” and “dermatitis contact” after the initiation of afatinib
for the treatment of metastatic lung adenocarcinoma. Cutaneous reactions are known adverse
reactions associated with afatinib treatment as described in the US prescribing information, and
therefore this event may not be related to bexagliflozin exposure. More events of rash led to
treatment discontinuation in the placebo group as compared to the bexagliflozin group of C-476
(1 event (<0.1%) versus 3 events (0.5%) in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups, respectively).
A time-to-event analysis showed that rash events appear to occur at a somewhat constant rate
over time (Figure 72). While data from other trials was limited, an imbalance unfavorable to
drug for events of rash as identified by FMQ was also observed in the placebo-controlled pool
(1.3% versus 0% in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups, respectively). No rash events in the
placebo-controlled pool were serious or lead to discontinuation from the trial.
A higher incidence of hypersensitivity events was observed in the bexagliflozin group (1.3%) as
compared to the placebo group (0.7%) in C-476. No events were serious or led to
discontinuation. Few events were captured in the FMQs for “Erythema,” “Urticaria,” and
“Anaphylactic reaction” limiting interpretability (Table 211).

FDA Reviewer Comment: Overall, an increased incidence of rash was observed in C-476 and
the placebo-controlled pool. This observation in combination with the Applicant’s description of
the rechallenge events may support drug relatedness. However, rash events were generally not
serious, nor did they typically lead to discontinuation. Skin reactions including rash and
urticaria have been identified in postmarketing reports for dapagliflozin and empagliflozin, and
these events are reported in the current US prescribing information for these products. Several
literature reports also seem to support the possibility of increased skin reactions associated with
SGLT2 inhibitor exposure (Damiani et al. 2016; Raschi et al. 2017; Sakaeda et al. 2018). It is
appropriate that information regarding rash be described in Section 6 of the labeling for
bexagliflozin. A Warnings and Precautions statement is not necessary to describe the rash events
observed, which, as noted above, were generally not serious nor did they typically lead to
discontinuation.
While no serious hypersensitivity or anaphylactic reactions were characterized, the frequency of
these events may be too low to evaluate in a premarket setting. Hypersensitivity to the drug and
any excipients is included as a contraindication in labeling for SGLT2 inhibitors, which also
appears appropriate for bexagliflozin.

17.3.5. Hepatic Injury
Hepatic injury events were evaluated as AESIs in the bexagliflozin clinical program to assess the
relationship between bexagliflozin and drug-induced liver injury (DILI). For any increase in
alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and/or aspartate aminotransferase (AST) >3x upper limit of
368

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

normal (ULN), investigators were instructed to confirm the hepatic enzyme elevation within 48
to 72 hours and to assess the clinical situation at least every 96 hours until ALT and/or AST
return to <2.5x ULN. Investigators were advised to stop study medication and report the event as
an AE if the enzyme elevation was confirmed or worsening. Hepatotoxicity was entered as an
AE should any of the following have occurred:
•
•
•

ALT or AST >8x ULN
ALT or AST >3x ULN and total bilirubin >2 x ULN or international normalized ratio
(INR) >1.5
ALT or AST >3x ULN with the appearance of fatigue, nausea, vomiting, right upper
quadrant pain or tenderness, fever, rash, and/or eosinophilia (>5%)

Investigators were instructed to permanently discontinue the study medication should a
hepatoxicity event occur. Note, the Applicant’s hepatotoxicity definition did not consider
elevations in ALP that could be indicative of cholestasis, and ALP was not systematically
assessed in all trials. For trials where ALP data were not available (i.e., C-448 and C-450), Hy’s
law analysis was completed irrespective of ALP information for the assessment of DILI.
The broad pool, which pools all data from phase 2b/3 trials utilizing the 20 mg dosage strength
(see Section 17.2 for additional information), was used to identify potential Hy’s Law cases
(Figure 73 and Table 212). No Hy’s Law cases were identified from available lab data. Enzyme
elevations in the cholestasis and Temple’s Corollary quadrants were generally balanced between
bexagliflozin and placebo groups (Table 212). There were 0.64 versus 1.08 subjects per 100
patient-years in the pooled bexagliflozin and placebo groups of the broad pool, respectively, that
experienced at least one hepatic injury event as assessed by FMQ. While there were no overall
concerning trends indicating DILI, selected narratives of subjects with outlier values were
reviewed. Narratives reviewed indicated potentially alternative etiologies and were inconclusive
(b) (6) was a 53-year-old Asian
with regards to drug relatedness. For example, Subject 476male assigned to the bexagliflozin group who had a history of hepatic steatosis, dyslipidemia,
cholelithiasis, and hepatitis B infection. The subject experienced a solitary elevation of ALT and
AST to 16.8x and 14.7x ULN, respectively, with an increase in total bilirubin to 1.29x ULN (this
subject represents the most extreme LFT observed in the clinical program as observed as the
rightmost point in Temple’s Corollary quadrant in Figure 73). In addition to chronic active
hepatitis B, the subject experienced recurrent infectious cholecystitis during the trial, indicating a
potential alternative etiology for the LFT elevations observed. Lastly, as discussed in Section
17.4, there were no noteworthy mean changes in bilirubin over time, and modest decreases in
mean ALT and AST in the placebo-controlled pool, and subjects with one or more liver
chemistry analyte values exceeding specified levels was also generally balanced between
treatment arms in the placebo-controlled pool, Trial C-476, and Trial C-448. Overall, there does
not appear to be a signal for DILI based on the information available.

17.3.6. DKA
DKA is a serious, acute complication of diabetes that may be potentially life-threatening. The
association between SGLT2 inhibitor treatment and increased risk of DKA was identified from
postmarketing reports for approved products while the clinical development program for
bexagliflozin was ongoing. A “DKA Monitoring and Adjudication Plan” was initiated January
2016 to enable relevant data to be captured and consistently adjudicated in the bexagliflozin
clinical program. Trials C-450, C-448, C-480, and C-476 were ongoing at the time the
monitoring plan was initiated. For these trials, protocols were updated via protocol amendments
to include DKA as an AESI, to describe the DKA monitoring procedures to investigators, and to
add a dedicated DKA case report form (CRF). These monitoring and documentation procedures
were incorporated into protocols for all trials initiated after January 2016 (i.e., C-419, C-423, C603). Trials C-418 and C-449 were completed prior to initiation of the DKA Monitoring and

370

Reference ID: 5112305

NDA 214373
Brenzavvy (bexagliflozin)

Adjudication Plan, and data from adverse event logs, SAE reports, and laboratory parameters
were used to identify potential DKA events retrospectively.
As detailed in the DKA Monitoring and Adjudication Plan, suspected DKA cases could be
identified based on subject-reported events, investigator-reported events, or adjudicatoridentified events. Subjects were informed of the signs and symptoms of DKA and were
instructed to call the study site and seek treatment should such signs or symptoms occur,
although ketone monitoring via home-use meter was not carried out in any trial. Investigators
were informed that DKA may occur in the absence of highly elevated plasma glucose (i.e.,
euglycemic DKA). DKA CRFs were to be completed by the investigators at all subject visits
(i.e., scheduled visits, unscheduled visits, phone consultations, etc.). The eCRFs were designed
to document whether a suspected DKA had occurred and to ensure the collection of relevant
clinical information to allow for adjudication. All potential DKA events underwent blinded
adjudication by an endocrinologist. In addition, a listing of SAEs was sent to the adjudicator to
identify DKA events potentially missed by investigators.
As shown in Table 213, DKA was categorized by the adjudicator as either confirmed DKA,
probable DKA, possible DKA, unlikely DKA, or intermediate DKA. The Applicant’s DKA
monitoring plan, including DKA case definitions, were discussed with the agency and agreed to
during IND interactions (see Written Response Only dated September 21, 2016 in DARRTS).
Table 213. DKA Case Definitions
Case Term
Case Definition
Confirmed DKA
An episode consisting of symptoms of DKA with ketonuria or ketonemia AND
one or more biochemical findings of acidosis, which responds to fluid
replenishment and, if needed, insulin
Probable DKA
An episode consisting of moderate/severe symptoms of DKA, which responds
to fluid replenishment and, if needed, insulin; OR
An episode consisting of moderate/severe symptoms of DKA with moderate to
large ketonuria and/or ketonemia without biochemical findings of acidosis,
which responds to fluid replenishment and, if needed, insulin
Possible DKA
An episode consisting of mild to moderate symptoms of DKA with small to
moderate ketonuria, condition self-managed, and resolves with or without
change in diabetic medications; OR
An episode of 4+ (40 mg/dL) or greater ketonuria with mild symptoms of DKA;
OR

The following definitions were specified in the DKA Monitoring and Adjudication Plan:
•

One or more biochemical findings of acidosis shall mean one or more of: (i) a venous pH
<7.3 or an arterial pH ≤7.3; (ii) a plasma bicarbonate <18 mM; or (iii) an albumincorrected anion gap >10 mM.

Symptoms of DKA shall mean exhibiting: (i) nausea with or without vomiting; (ii)
abdominal pain; (iii) tachypnea and/or hyperpnea; (v) polydipsia; (vi) polyuria; (vii)
malaise and/or asthenia; or (viii) confusion and/or disorientation.
Ketonuria shall mean a moderate or large urine nitroprusside reaction (color matches or
exceeds 3+ or 40 mg/dL sample swatch for test strip).
Ketonemia shall mean a capillary blood β-hydroxybutyrate concentration >3.0 mM or a
moderate or large plasma nitroprusside reaction (>1.0 mM).

Overall, 19 suspected events were sent for adjudication (Table 214). There were few confirmed
DKA events in the clinical program for bexagliflozin (two and three confirmed DKA events in
bexagliflozin and placebo-exposed subjects, respectively). In trial C-476, there were more events
of confirmed DKA in the placebo group as compared to the bexagliflozin group (bexagliflozin:
one event (0.09%), placebo: three events (0.5%)). This imbalance became increasingly favorable
to drug when events adjudicated as confirmed or probable DKA were pooled (bexagliflozin: one
event (0.09%), placebo: five events (0.9%)). Only one event of confirmed DKA occurred in the
remainder of the bexagliflozin program in Trial C-419 (bexagliflozin: one event (0.6%), placebo:
zero events (0%).
Narratives for the two confirmed DKA events in the bexagliflozin group were reviewed. Subject
(b) (6) was a 37-year-old white male enrolled in C-419. The narrative reports that the
419subject presented to the hospital on Day 33 with epigastric pain, nausea, dehydration, and emesis
following a binge drinking episode. The subject's severe anion gap metabolic acidosis was
accompanied by high blood lactic acid levels, thought to be exacerbated by hypoperfusion and
possible sepsis as well as concurrent metformin use. The discharge diagnosis was DKA/lactic
(b) (6) was a 40-year-old
acidosis secondary to alcohol abuse and metformin. Subject 476white female enrolled in C-476. On Day 39, the subject presented to the hospital with diffuse
abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting and diarrhea, and was hospitalized. The subject was
rehydrated with intravenous fluid plus bicarbonate.
As discussed in Section 17.4, transient decreases in mean serum bicarbonate occurred in the
placebo-controlled pool starting at Week 2 and recovering by Week 18, with a maximum mean
decrease of -1.4 mEq/L in the bexagliflozin group compared to -0.5 mEq/L in the placebo group
at Week 6. Transient decreases in serum bicarbonate were also observed in C-476 and C-448
although to a lesser extent. In line with these results, there were also more subjects who
experienced at least one level 1 (<20 mEq/L), 2 (<18 mEq/L), and 3 (<15 mEq/L) low
bicarbonate values in the bexagliflozin group of the placebo-controlled pool compared to the
placebo group. As few events of DKA occurred in the clinical program, the meaningfulness of
this laboratory data is unclear.
FDA Reviewer Comment: The DKA monitoring and assessment procedures appear adequate to
have identified and consistently classified events. While the small number of DKA events in the
clinical program limits interpretability, it is reassuring that an excess of DKA events was not
observed with bexagliflozin exposure. As with other SGLT2 inhibitors, premarket programs are
unlikely to adequately assess DKA risk, and class labeling appears warranted.

17.3.7. Renal Events
AKI, secondary to volume depletion in the setting of SGLT2 inhibitor treatment, is currently
described in the Warnings and Precautions section of the US prescribing information across the
class. While postmarketing events of AKI have been observed for approved SGLT2 inhibitors,
there is increasing evidence that SGLT2 inhibitor treatment may be renal protective in patients
with chronic kidney disease, slowing the decline in eGFR and progression to end-stage renal
disease, and reducing renal-related mortality, independent of its effects on glycemic control
(Gilbert and Thorpe 2019; Menne et al. 2019; Sridhar et al. 2020). In this section, renal function
laboratory data and renal events, including AKI, are reviewed.

Renal Function Tests
Initiation of SGLT2 inhibitor treatment causes modest increases in serum creatinine and
decreases in eGFR, an effect that is also observed with bexagliflozin. Maximum changes in
serum creatinine and eGFR generally occur within six to twelve weeks of starting therapy and
then stabilize. In the placebo-controlled pool, enrolling subjects primarily with normal renal
function, changes in serum creatinine and eGFR were generally similar between treatment
groups (Figure 74). However, changes in renal function were variable depending on the subject
population evaluated. As discussed in Section 7.7.2, in C-448 enrolling subjects with stage 3
CKD, serum creatinine increased 0.14 mg/dL (10.8%) and eGFR decreased -4.6 mL/min/1.73 m2
from baseline to Week 6 in the bexagliflozin group, as compared to an increase in serum
creatinine of 0.01 mg/dL (0.7%) and a decrease in eGFR of -0.08 mL/min/1.73 m2.

decreases in eGFR of -3.1 mL/min/1.73 m2 from baseline to Week 12), which stabilizes after
approximately 12 weeks of treatment. However, with prolonged bexagliflozin exposure, there

Renal Events
AKI events, as identified by the AKI FMQ, that occurred in C-476 are shown in Table 216.
Renal failure events were prespecified as AESIs in the protocol for this trial, although no special
procedures were implemented to identify or appropriately classify or document these events. A
higher incidence of AKI events was observed in the bexagliflozin group (2.2% of subjects) than
the placebo group (1.6% of subjects). However, there was no imbalance in AKI events between
groups when the analysis was limited to serious events, with 0.8% and 0.7% of subjects
experiencing events in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups; respectively. Three subjects
(0.26%) assigned to the bexagliflozin group and two subjects (0.35%) assigned to the placebo
group stopped dosing as a result of an AKI event. A random selection of narratives from serious
AKI events were reviewed. While some AKI events occurred in the setting of
hypotension/volume depletion, alternative etiologies that could have contributed to or caused the
events were also present.
No AKI events occurred in the bexagliflozin groups of the placebo-controlled pool or activecontrolled trials. AKI events occurring in C-448 enrolling subjects with moderate renal
impairment are reviewed in Section 7.7.2.
FDA Reviewer Comment: As discussed in the next section, SGLT2 inhibitor treatment, including
treatment with bexagliflozin, is associated with volume depletion and hypotension, and current
labeling for approved SGLT2 inhibitors describes the potential risk of AKI secondary to volume
depletion as Warnings and Precautions. Similar labeling appears appropriate for bexagliflozin.

17.3.8. Volume Depletion/Hypotension Events
SGLT2 inhibitors are associated with osmotic diuresis and thus volume depletion and
hypotension events are of concern for this drug class. FMQs for “Volume Depletion,”
“Hypotension,” “Dizziness,” and “Syncope” were utilized to characterize the impact of osmotic
diuresis in subjects treated with bexagliflozin.
An imbalance unfavorable to drug in hypotension events was observed, with 48 (4.2%) and 15
(2.6%) subjects experiencing a hypotension event in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups of C476, respectively. All events were nonserious, with one event in a bexagliflozin-treated subject
leading to treatment discontinuation. While events of dizziness may be nonspecific to volume
depletion events, an excess of these events in the bexagliflozin group was also observed (7.3%
versus 5.1% of subjects in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups having an event, respectively).
Twelve (1.1%) and four subjects (0.7%) experienced as least one volume depletion event in the
bexagliflozin and placebo groups, respectively. All events identified were events of dehydration.
One dehydration event in the bexagliflozin group led to treatment discontinuation. There were
more serious dehydration events in the placebo group as compared to the bexagliflozin group.
AKI is a potentially serious consequence of volume depletion. Three of the 12 subjects who
experienced dehydration in the bexagliflozin group had six events of AKI, of which two were
SAEs. No events of AKI were observed in the four subjects who experienced dehydration in the
placebo group. See Section 17.3.7 for additional discussion regarding AKI events. There were
more subjects who experienced a syncope event in the placebo group than the bexagliflozin
group. Events related to volume depletion events were limited in other trials, limiting
interpretability. Overall, labeling similar to other members of the drug class appears appropriate.

17.3.9. UTI Including Pyelonephritis and Urosepsis
Treatment with SGLT2 inhibitors increases the risk for UTIs due to increased glucosuria. Serious
UTIs including pyelonephritis and urosepsis requiring hospitalization have been reported in
subjects receiving SGLT2 inhibitors, and the risk of pyelonephritis and urosepsis is currently
included as a Warnings and Precautions statement in the US prescribing information for
approved products. These events were considered AESIs in the bexagliflozin clinical program.
UTI events occurring in the placebo-controlled pool are discussed in Section 7.6.1.3. The FMQ
for “Renal and Urinary Tract Infection” was utilized to query for UTI events in C-476. In
accordance with data from the placebo-controlled pool, there were more events of UTI in the
bexagliflozin group (148 [13.1%] subjects with at least one event) as compared to the placebo
group (68 [12.0%]). There was a minor imbalance that was unfavorable to bexagliflozin for UTIrelated AEs leading to treatment discontinuation with bexagliflozin (0.8%) as compared to
placebo (0.5%). There was no meaningful imbalance in events of pyelonephritis or urosepsis in
the bexagliflozin group (11 [1.0%] subjects with at least one event) as compared to the placebo
group (6 [1.1%]) as determined by a grouped query of the following PTs: pyelonephritis,
pyelonephritis acute, urosepsis, kidney infection. As described in Section 7.6.1.3, upon review of
SAE narratives for sepsis events, four events of sepsis in the bexagliflozin group and one event
of sepsis in the placebo group appeared secondary to UTI. Addition of sepsis events to the
analysis for pyelonephritis and urosepsis did not significantly change the interpretation of results.
Regarding the remainder of the phase 3 program, only one other event of pyelonephritis occurred
in a bexagliflozin treated subject in C-480.
FDA Reviewer Comment: Overall, the small number of events of pyelonephritis and urosepsis
limit interpretability of the data, although it is reassuring that no meaningful imbalances were
observed in the available data. As increased risk of pyelonephritis and urosepsis is considered a
class effect, labeling similar to other approved products appears appropriate.

17.3.10. GMI
Treatment with SGLT2 inhibitors, including bexagliflozin, increases the risk for GMI. GMI is
proposed for inclusion in the labeling by the Applicant, both as an a commonly occurring
adverse reaction and as a Warnings and Precautions. GMI in the placebo-controlled pool is
discussed in Section 7.6.1.3. The following section focus on clinically meaningful events of GMI
in phase 3 trials not included in the placebo-controlled pool.
All phase 3 trials demonstrated a higher incidence of GMI in the bexagliflozin group as
compared to comparator groups. In C-476, 9.0% and 2.8% of subjects had at least one event of
GMI in the bexagliflozin and placebo groups, respectively, as identified by a customized
Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities query (CMQ) developed by the Applicant (Table
219). GMIs occurred throughout the study with an onset from 3 to 1129 days after randomization
(b) (6) ) in the bexagliflozin group experienced an
(Applicant’s analysis). One subject (476episode of severe vulvovaginal candidiasis that was not considered an SAE by the investigator.
No SAEs of GMI were reported, although 14 subjects (1.2%), all in the bexagliflozin group,
ceased dosing due to GMI (Applicant’s analysis). The Applicant reports that five male subjects
developed phimosis in C-476, four (0.4%) in the bexagliflozin group and one (0.2%) in the
placebo group, and surgery/circumcision was performed or planned for all subjects in the
bexagliflozin group. Information describing phimosis potentially leading to circumcision should
be included in the labeling.

17.3.11. Hypoglycemia
Insulin and insulin secretagogues (e.g., sulfonylureas) are known to cause hypoglycemia. The
risk of hypoglycemia may be increased when an SGLT2 inhibitor is used in combination with
insulin or insulin secretagogues, and this risk is currently labeled as a Warning and Precaution in
approved SGLT2 inhibitor drugs and will be similarly included in the bexagliflozin labeling. In
all clinical trials, subjects were provided with a glucometer and diary for recording blood glucose
measurements and signs and symptoms that may be related to hypoglycemia. Subjects were also
advised to perform daily fasting blood glucose measurements and record results. Hypoglycemia
was defined in the clinical program into the following five categories: severe hypoglycemia,
documented symptomatic hypoglycemia, asymptomatic hypoglycemia, probable symptomatic
hypoglycemia, and relative hypoglycemia. Severe hypoglycemia was defined as an event
requiring third party assistance for recovery. All other hypoglycemia categories relied on blood
glucose cutoff of 70 mg/dL (level 1 hypoglycemia). As 70 mg/dL is generally considered an alert
value where action should be taken to prevent further reductions in blood glucose, a re-analysis
of the trial data was requested to assess level 2 hypoglycemia using the cutoff of <54 mg/dL
(American Diabetes Association Professional Practice et al. 2022a).
Severe hypoglycemia and level 2 hypoglycemia in placebo-controlled studies is presented in
Table 220. Events rates for severe hypoglycemia and level 2 hypoglycemia were low in all
placebo-controlled trials, generally occurring in trials where background insulin or sulfonylurea
use was allowed. While it is reassuring that excess hypoglycemia was not observed with
bexagliflozin, limited data impedes meaningful interpretation.
